## 9. Open questions for G0 — all ruled 2026-09-01

Kept for the record; the rulings are in §10 under "G0 bulk stamp".

1. ~~**License.**~~ → MIT OR Apache-2.0 dual (§1.5). Directory and GitHub repo were already renamed to `lastcall` before G0.
2. ~~**Spec home.**~~ → committed in full at `docs/spec/` (nothing in the corpus is private).
3. ~~**Ack semantics.**~~ → local ack + explicit jump-to-agent (§5.7, §6.6).
4. ~~**Launch outside a configured parent dir.**~~ → ad-hoc watch of cwd with a notice (§6.1).
5. ~~**Accept-all confirmation.**~~ → confirm only above 10 files (§6.7); hardcoded in v1.
6. ~~**Debounce default.**~~ → 750 ms, hardcoded in v1 (§6.5).
7. ~~**Ledger anchoring model.**~~ → seen-tree model (§10, decided earlier the same day).
8. ~~**Agent-status strategy.**~~ → per-pane subscriptions + resync ratified as the §5.4 contract.

No question is left `[OPEN]`-pending-data. Items 5 and 6 are re-examined at the Phase 4 circle-back (§3.5).

## 10. Decision log

(append-only; entries: date, question, verbatim ruling, tag `[OPEN]`/`[LEANING]`/`[DECIDED]`, supersedes-pointer if any)

- **2026-09-01 — Project name** `[DECIDED]`: **lastcall**. Ruling (sponsor, in session): "lastcall makes sense." Context: "ledgr" is namespace-crowded on GitHub (multiple active finance apps) and semantically claimed by accounting tools; "accptr" is unique but hard to spell (double-c, dropped vowels); themed alternatives (`onceover`, `signoff`, `finalcut`, `shipcheck`, `lgtm`) are all taken by established projects; `lastcall` had only trivial unrelated collisions and matches the product framing — the last call before code ships. Two-way door until the repo goes public in Phase 1.
- **2026-09-01 — Ledger baseline model** `[DECIDED]`: **seen-tree model** — the baseline is a content snapshot (a git tree in a private object store plus per-path overrides); HEAD is never consulted for correctness; git history only annotates/groups (upstream group row, "includes upstream changes" badge, transition notices). Supersedes the original "blob at current HEAD" default rule (review F1/F2/F4: an agent commit hid its own work; ancestry could not distinguish agent commits from upstream pulls; rebase/branch-switch killed accepts) and the interim "reviewed anchor + sticky local-commit set" proposal presented the same day, which the sponsor and orchestrator judged to be trading hook complexity for worse git complexity. Sponsor ruling (verbatim, in session): "yes, I agree with you." One-way-ish door: the on-disk schema is built around it; revisit only with a schema bump.
- **2026-09-01 — Agent hooks** `[DECIDED]`: hooks are an **additive attribution layer**, not the change-detection foundation (§6.8). Sponsor ruling (verbatim): "hooks can be an extra layer of metadata and attribution, and then we can determine at a future point just how much effort it is and the value we get from it ... still move forward with adding hook support at a later point in the phases." Rationale recorded: hooks cannot see Bash-mediated changes (formatters, generators, sed, package managers) without a filesystem diff anyway; agent coverage would require per-agent integrations (herdr's model, not ours); the seen-tree model removed most of the git complexity that motivated the question. Two-way door.
- **2026-09-01 — G0 bulk stamp** `[DECIDED]`: sponsor ruling (verbatim, post-compaction message): "if you don't have any questions, you can take my stamp and apply G0 then start phase 1 kickoff." The orchestrator had presented the §9 defaults in bulk before compaction and had no questions; every default is adopted as recommended:
  - **Q1 License:** MIT OR Apache-2.0 dual. Two-way door until the first external contribution.
  - **Q2 Spec home:** the full corpus is committed at `docs/spec/` (this file, `01-scenarios.md`, later kickoff specs at `9N-phaseN-kickoff.md`); `z_ignore/` keeps only the session handoff. Two-way door.
  - **Q3 Ack semantics:** local ack + explicit jump-to-agent (`agent.focus`), because herdr has no mark-seen API (§5.7). Two-way door; Phase 4/5 circle-back.
  - **Q4 Launch outside a configured parent dir:** watch the launch cwd ad hoc with a notice; never error. Two-way door.
  - **Q5 Accept-all confirmation:** confirm only when more than 10 files are affected; threshold hardcoded in v1. Two-way door; Phase 4 circle-back.
  - **Q6 Debounce:** 750 ms, hardcoded in v1 (no config key). Two-way door; Phase 4 circle-back.
  - **Q8 Agent-status strategy:** per-pane `pane.agent_status_changed` subscriptions + resync is the §5.4 contract. One-way-ish: the client topology is built around it; revisit only if herdr ships a global status stream.
  - **Editorial fixes made while stamping** (orchestrator, no semantic change): `[herdr]` became a TOML table with `mode`/`session` (§6.1); fixture repos are script-generated rather than vendored tarballs (§8 Phase 1); Phase 1's CI gate item carries its split interpretation; toolchain and herdr-fetch facts recorded in §4.1/§4.4; `lastcall-testkit` named as the third crate (§4.1).
  - **§11 deferral ledger:** the seven seeded candidates are confirmed as standing entries under this stamp.
- **2026-09-01 — Phase 1 rulings** (presented at close-out; each `[LEANING]` becomes `[DECIDED]` when the sponsor merges the Phase 1 PR or replies otherwise):
  - **Protocol 20 vs 21** `[DECIDED]` (sponsor, 2026-09-01, verbatim: "rec plz", after a full rec-vs-alt impact walk-through): accept both (Amendment v1.1) rather than pin 20 only — the consumed surface is byte-identical on both and the next herdr release will ship 21; the engine never depends on the socket, so the worst case is degraded herdr extras, never review correctness. Alternative on record: pin 20 and bump at the Phase 5 circle-back. **Attached roadmap amendment (§3.4):** the scheduled herdr-compat workflow moves from Phase 9 to Phase 5, so a 21 release is diffed against our consumed-surface fixture within a day of shipping — this closes rec's one blind spot (a herdr upgrade on the sponsor's machine ahead of our pin). Two-way door.
  - **Status-connection teardown on agent release** `[DECIDED]` (PR #1 merged `692e43a`; re-tagged 2026-09-02): accept the worker's additive extension (Amendment v1.1 item 3). Alternative: keep the stream for the pane's lifetime. Two-way door.
  - **Informational, no ruling needed:** the worker fast-forwarded its harness-created branch from `main` onto `feat/phase1-scaffold` (pure ff, verified); per-pane generation counters (a `pane.get` invalidates only that pane's in-flight status events); `hello-herdr --socket` exits 0 on peer disconnect (documented); XDG resolution is hand-rolled through the injected `Env`.
  - **Phase 2 unit-test floor: 101** (engine 85, testkit 16, bin 0), recorded here; ratchets only upward.
- **2026-09-01 — Phase 2 kickoff rulings** `[DECIDED]` (sponsor, verbatim: "1 rec 2 explain more 3 rec 4 explain more", then "rec on both"):
  - **D4 case-only rename on macOS:** keep the frozen scenario and satisfy it mechanically — on a case-insensitive root the current-side existence of an index/override path is a byte-exact `readdir` of its parent (cached per scan), and `ls-files --others` runs with `-c core.ignorecase=false`; result `f.txt` deleted + `F.txt` added for both root kinds. Alternative on record: draft roots show the addition only and D4 is amended. Two-way door.
  - **Draft-glob semantics:** a relative `draft_dirs` entry matches directories under each configured parent dir **and** under each discovered git root; the matched directory is the root; its untracked files leave the enclosing git root's candidates, tracked content stays the git root's. Alternative: parent dirs only. Two-way door.
  - **`lastcall watch` subcommand** is in Phase 2 scope as the sponsor-visible surface for transition notices (`--exit-after`, and `--poll` added during review for hosts without filesystem events). Alternative: `status` only. Two-way door.
  - **Unanchored store objects:** git never physically writes an object the alternate already has, so the seen tree lives in the user's objects dir and can be pruned after a history rewrite plus reflog expiry; ruling = fail open (a missing seen tree is `null` for the session with a notice; the next accept-all refolds from empty), never `gc`/`prune`/`repack` the store, hardening deferred to §11. Alternative: copy tree objects into the store at first sight and every fold (~a day of scope, fragile alternate toggling). Two-way door.
- **2026-09-02 — Phase 2 close-out** (ratified `[DECIDED]` by the sponsor merging PR #2, `ba62907`, 2026-09-02):
  - **Unreadable or foreign ledger opens with nothing seen** `[DECIDED]`: an unparsable ledger, an unknown schema major, or a ledger whose recorded `root` is not the opened root is moved aside (`ledger.json.unreadable-<secs>-<n>`) and the root opens with `seen_tree = null` (everything pending, invariant 2) — deliberately *not* first sight at the current HEAD, which would hide every commit since the original first sight. Alternative: first sight. Two-way door.
  - **`open` reads a present ledger without the lock** `[DECIDED]`: `status` never blocks behind a long fold; writes still lock, re-load and merge. Alternative: lock-first open with a longer retry. Two-way door.
  - **`watch` publishes a pile per root per scan even when unchanged** `[DECIDED]`: per the kickoff ("Pile after every scan"); Phase 3 may publish only on change. Two-way door.
  - **Informational, no ruling needed:** D6 hides a *deleted* skip-worktree file (git semantics; the filter can only suppress absent paths); the B8 best-effort "stashed" notice is not implemented (reset/checkout notices cover the observable transitions); `--poll <secs>` sets both polling backstops; worker spend projected above the $3 flag and below the $5 stop (two verifier passes plus a filesystem-event investigation; no meter available to the worker).
  - **Phase 3 unit-test floor: 182** (engine 166, testkit 16, bin 0), recorded here; ratchets only upward.
- **2026-09-02 — Phase 3 kickoff rulings** (sponsor, verbatim "1 rec 2 rec 3 rec 4 rec 5 alt i think alternate keybindings are nice feature 6 i'm more concerned about compaction and loss of context on the spec and less so on cost"; kickoff `docs/spec/92-phase3-kickoff.md`, design review 41 findings folded):
  - **Bare `lastcall` launches the TUI** `[DECIDED]`; `lastcall tui [--poll N]` is the explicit spelling; a non-TTY stdout exits 2 with a hint. Alternative: subcommand stays mandatory. Two-way door.
  - **Nav lists a root iff its pile has rows** `[DECIDED]`; the in-progress tag decorates but never lists; the attention flag is Phase 5's. Alternative: list in-progress roots too. Two-way door.
  - **First frame is the empty state with `scanning…`** `[DECIDED]`, piles fill in as the watcher's first scans publish. Alternative: synchronous scan before the first draw. Two-way door.
  - **`Esc` never quits** `[DECIDED]` (closes help, else focus to nav); `q`/Ctrl-C quit. Alternative: Esc quits from the nav. Two-way door.
  - **`[keys]` config table in Phase 3** `[DECIDED — alternative taken]`: the sponsor chose configurable keybindings over a constant table ("alternate keybindings are nice feature"); §6.1 gains a `[keys]` table via Amendment v1.3 (proposed below, ratified by merging the Phase 3 PR); shape in the kickoff, deliverable 5. Two-way door.
  - **Phase built by three sequential workers** `[DECIDED]` (3a engine+state+render+snapshots, 3b input+loop+CLI+parity, 3c PTY+docs+report): the sponsor's concern is a worker losing the spec to context compaction, not cost; per-worker spend band $1–2, tripwire $5. Alternative: one worker. Two-way door.
  - **Sponsor direction, not yet a ruling (2026-09-02):** herdr exposes workspaces, tabs and panes programmatically, and a lastcall pane living inside a workspace scoped to one repo should show only that repo, not the whole parent dir — the Phase 5 kickoff evaluates a per-pane root filter driven by herdr workspace metadata (verify the workspace surface against the pinned release tag first, §5 rule). Recorded so the Phase 3 state model keeps roots filterable (the nav is already a function of `App.roots`).
- **2026-09-02 — Phase 4 kickoff rulings** `[DECIDED]` (kickoff `93-phase4-kickoff.md`, after the adversarial design review — 19 findings folded, six gating: engine-global scan sequence, live confirm counts, row-cap priority partition, S2 bench collapse size, multi-root accept effect, advance-with-wrap; sponsor, verbatim "1 rec 2 rec 3 rec 4 rec 5 rec I generally think that that is accurate, um, and that the recommendations are good"):
  - **Per-root row cap 10,000** `[DECIDED]`: `EngineOptions.row_cap`, override and flagged paths always materialised, other candidates in path order until the cap, `Pile.omitted` counts the non-priority candidates not hashed, notice `<shown> files shown · <omitted> more changed paths not scanned (first 10,000 by path)`. Alternative: 5,000. Two-way door (a number and a sentence).
  - **Accept keys `a` / `shift-a` / `ctrl-a`** `[DECIDED]` with `y`/`Enter` confirm and `n`/`Esc` cancel inside the modal only. Alternative: `a` / `shift-a` / `*` for multiplexers that eat `ctrl-a`; the `[keys]` table covers that user. Two-way door.
  - **A second accept while one is in flight is ignored** `[DECIDED]` (status `accept in progress`; the confirm modal never opens while one is in flight). Alternative: queue one. Two-way door.
  - **Three sequential workers** `[DECIDED]` (4a engine, 4b TUI, 4c e2e + bench + docs), the Phase 3 shape. Alternative: two. Operational.
  - **Header Accept All is global** `[DECIDED]`: `ctrl-a` and the header control fold every listed root under one lock hold, confirm `across <R> repos`; the per-root fold is `a` on the root's nav entry (confirm `in <root>`). Alternative: current root only. Two-way door. The sponsor asked at ruling time how the two are told apart: selection, modal text and the selection-dependent hint; a dedicated per-repo key is a post-build ruling candidate. *Superseded 2026-09-14 (Phase 10 deliverable 7, §10 that day): the per-root fold is `A` (`accept_file`) on the root's nav entry and on a group entry; `a` there refuses with a status naming that key.*
- **2026-09-02 — Phase 3 close-out** (sponsor, verbatim "1 rec 2 rec 3 rec 4 rec 5 rec 6 rec"; ratified `[DECIDED]` by the sponsor merging PR #3):
  - **PTY live-update gate stays "≤ 1.75 s, min of two tries"** `[DECIDED]`: met (≈ 1.0 s); the first try lands on the 1 s poll backstop (1.1–1.8 s) on a host without file events and is recorded here as the honest expectation. Alternative: min of three or a 2.5 s budget. Two-way door; **trigger:** one CI flake of `pty_live_update_hunk_nav_click_and_quit` amends the gate to min of three (§3.4).
  - **`[keys]` specs are case-folded** `[DECIDED]`: `K` ≡ `k`, an uppercase binding is spelled `shift-k`, ctrl chords are case-insensitive; the help overlay shows the canonical spelling. Alternative: case-sensitive bare letters (vim-style `Q` = shift-q). Two-way door.
  - **An empty key list is an error, not an unbind** `[DECIDED]`: `quit = []` fails uniformly for every action, so no action can be stranded without a key. Alternative: `[]` unbinds any action except `quit`/`help`. Two-way door.
  - **The status line shows the full watched path** `[DECIDED]` (the header keeps the basename); a status notice now expires after 30 s (`app::STATUS_TTL`, review F1) and the key hints return. Alternative: basename in both places. Two-way door.
  - **Refresh-vs-watcher pile ordering deferred** `[DECIDED]`: `r` scans on a local channel, so an older refresh pile can briefly overwrite a newer watcher pile until the next scan; entered in §11 below. Alternative: route `r` through the watcher now. Two-way door.
  - **Kickoff deliverable 4's `tests/test_perf_tui.rs` struck** `[DECIDED]`: the "50 000-line diff renders under 16 ms" check was never a gate and no test was written; the windowed rendering (`hunk_offsets`/`diff_len`, only the visible lines are built) is the implementation. Alternative: an `#[ignore]` timing test. Two-way door.
  - **Worker deviations from the kickoff, recorded (informational):** 3a — the too-small text is `too small: 40×10 min` (the kickoff's wording cannot fit at 30 columns), the mode-change scene mutates `f3` not `f1`, `Action::Back` was added for `h`/Esc, `App::apply` returns `(Changed, Option<Effect>)`, a directory replacing a file renders as `Typechange`, hints narrow below 70 columns, `range_label` omits `,1`; 3b — `App::move_selection` is `pub` (the wheel over the nav needs the hit map, which lives in the loop), `Local::Notice` and (review F4) `Local::Fatal` variants beyond the kickoff's three, `just probe-tui` leaves its temp fixture in place so the printed exports are re-runnable, the quit-path watcher timeout was built outside the runtime and panicked on `q` (caught by 3b's own PTY drive, fixed `f1ce23e`); 3c — `vt100` is a normal dependency of the testkit (kickoff wording), no fix was needed from the PTY scenes. Review findings not fixed by design: a wrong-typed `[keys]` value fails every command like any other config field while a semantically bad one fails only `tui`/`config` (F2, message made friendly); `remote_slug("host:~user/repo")` keeps the `~user` segment (F15); crossterm falls back to `tput` on a 0×0 window and the TUI survives it (F12).
  - **Phase 4 unit-test floor: 255** (lastcall lib 64, bin 4, engine 168, testkit 19), recorded here; ratchets only upward.
- **2026-09-02 — Phase 4 close-out rulings** `[DECIDED]` (build-surfaced; verifier (a) 8 findings, 6 folded `33dc96a`; verifier (b) 6 findings, 5 folded `863e739`; sponsor, verbatim "1 rec but i'm curious is our architecture / process can't actually achieve better performance ---- can you clarify if this means that my current 50 clones and opening lastcall for the first time and if I had 4000 hunk diffs, then this would take 57 seconds to render?  if so... that's not a good success strategy if we don't know root cause and if it's easy to fix or not 2 rec 3 can we move some to prepush hook instead and then you can do pushes in the future on your own? 4 rec 5 rec 6 rec and yes we need to ensure that a deferral task is to ensure that we limit to 1 instance or otherwise handle this gracefully with N-instances somehow...."; on the revised item 1 and item 3, verbatim "1rec 3 honestly I think maybe we should split between test-unit and test-integration (if not done already), and then if we do that, is test-unit faster if it's really just code, and test-integration does more of the in-depth probes or otehr work?  if so, then I could keep push to myself, but then you could run the test-integration as a standaring worker rule at the end of its work (or you could run at any time as orch.)"):
  - **S1 open/scan cost becomes a Phase 5 entry obligation** `[DECIDED — revised after sponsor challenge]`: `Engine::open` and `scan_all` iterate roots serially and each root costs 29 git subprocesses at open plus 19 per scan at ≈13 ms each (bench S1); hunk count is irrelevant (S2 opens a 100,000-line diff in 12 ms). For the sponsor's workload (50 clones, 4,000 rows) that is ≈16 s of empty screen and ≈13 s of progressive fill, not 57 s. Root cause is known and contained (serial loop + per-root call list: several `config` reads at store open, separate HEAD/upstream/index calls). The orchestrator first proposed Phase 9; the sponsor's challenge ("not a good success strategy if we don't know root cause") is right, so Phase 5 gains the entry obligation in §8 (≤ 6 s first frame at 50 roots / 4,000 rows; ≥ 4× on S1). Alternative: a standalone mini-phase before Phase 5. Two-way door.
  - **Every on-screen count uses thousands separators** `[DECIDED]`: `10,000+ files` in header and nav, matching the cap notice; one formatter shared by engine and TUI. Alternative: bare digits. Two-way door.
  - **Fast unit tier, deeper pre-push tier, push stays human** `[DECIDED]`: store-backed proptests run 8 cases in `just test-unit` (`PROPTEST_CASES` override), 64 in `just test-prepush` and CI; `test-prepush` also runs the integration tier and is a standing worker rule (§4.4) plus a pre-push hook backstop. The sponsor keeps every push ("I could keep push to myself"). Alternative: lift the push deny for feature branches. Two-way door.
  - **Hunk-accept status says what is left** `[DECIDED]`: `accepted f1 · 2 hunks left` / `… · 1 hunk left` / `… · file complete`; whole-file accept stays `accepted f1`. Alternative: `hunk 1 of N` (the cursor slot, always 1). Two-way door.
  - **Rescan failure after a committed accept surfaces as Err** `[DECIDED]` → §11 (verifier (a) F4).
  - **Shared `index.tmp` and multiple instances** `[DECIDED]` → §11 with the sponsor's addition: the hardening must either limit lastcall to one instance per state dir or handle N instances gracefully; trigger = Phase 5 kickoff.

- **2026-09-04 — Gate 5 sponsor run: diagnosis and Phase 6 agenda** `[DECIDED]` (sponsor, verbatim "1 rec 2 rec 3 rec 4 rec - great debugging - thanks", then on the nav bug "taht can be for phase 6, btw --- I also merged the PR already so we're ready to proceed"):
  - **"No changes until the very end, then everything at once" is the agent, not lastcall — informational.** The sponsor's Claude Code session wrote each batch with one shell command (a `sed` loop over every file; the batch-3 command ran 03:21:50.99–03:21:51.24 UTC and every edited file plus `.git/index` carries that second as mtime), so the disk changed once per batch and lastcall showed the batch about a second later. Three live probes on the Phase 5 release build — `lastcall watch` over a temp repo, `lastcall watch` over the sponsor's real parent dir with appends to the real lastcall README, and the TUI in a scripted PTY — each showed a pile about one second after every edit at a two-second cadence, in-place and atomic-rename alike. The one real limit: the edit debounce is trailing-edge, so a writer that never pauses 750 ms postpones the scan until it does (six seconds of edits 300 ms apart → one pile 630 ms after the burst; agenda item below).
  - **Start-up "stutter" is one draw per arriving pile, not repeated discovery — draw coalescing → Phase 6 agenda** `[DECIDED]`: discovery runs once, before the screen opens (about two seconds for the sponsor's 18 roots, a blank terminal meanwhile); then the first root's pile draws alone, the other 17 piles arrive from the pool inside ~100 ms and each redraws the nav, then the watch install lands and the `watching` notice draws. A frame capture also counted 85 no-op draws in the first seven seconds: every herdr resync emits `PaneAssociation` per pane, `rederives` re-derives, and `HerdrUpdate::Roots` reports `Changed::Yes` unconditionally. Fix shape: drain every ready channel, then draw once per loop pass; `Roots` reports a change only when the derived map differs. Alternative: fix on the Phase 5 branch before merge. Two-way door.
  - **A `discovering…` frame before `Engine::open` → Phase 6 agenda** `[DECIDED]`. Alternative: accept the blank seconds (they scale with root count only). Two-way door.
  - **Debug tracing on the watcher, scanner and draw loop → Phase 6 agenda** `[DECIDED]`: `LASTCALL_LOG=debug` produced only herdr-client lines because those paths carry no `tracing` calls at all; the diagnosis needed three probes instead of one log. Alternative: none worth taking.
  - **Debounce starvation cap → Phase 6 agenda, undecided** `[DECIDED]`: a forced scan every few seconds during a burst would show partial progress; the alternative — one coalesced pile at the end of a burst is what a reviewer wants to read — stays open for the kickoff.
  - **Nav scroll offset is not persistent → Phase 6 agenda** `[DECIDED]` (sponsor-reported during the run: clicking `ledger.rs` mid-list moved it to the bottom row): `render_nav` recomputes the window every frame as `offset = selected + 1 − rows` whenever the selected line is past the first page, so any selection change re-anchors the selected row at the bottom and a click on a visible row scrolls the list. Fix shape: `App::nav_top` carried frame to frame through the `HitMap`, moved only when the selection leaves the window; the first jump past a page still lands on the bottom row, so the existing snapshots hold. Alternative: fix now on the Phase 5 branch. Two-way door.

I'm glad we're doing the 3s cap like this and yeah I hadn't though of D but that could be really gnarly and it's a completely realistic real-world scenario for someone running an app at debug logging in a repo"). Kickoff `95-phase6-kickoff.md`, design review 15 findings (7 gating) folded before the rulings.
  - **Collapsed rows expand on demand** `[DECIDED]`: `e` / `[e expand]` on a `Glob` or `Size` row, hunks computed off the UI task from the row's own blob ids, capped at 2,000 lines, held outside `row.hunks` so accept stays whole-row (§6.3 "single accept" intact); binary rows never expand. Alternative: reword the placeholder and let Phase 8 decide. Two-way door.
  - **Debounce starvation cap: 3 s, hardcoded, with Amendment v1.6 to §6.5** `[DECIDED]`: the first event of a burst is remembered and the trailing-edge deadline never slides past first event + 3 s; a never-quiet root is scanned about every 3.2 s instead of never (today only the 30 s backstop breaks the starvation — and a noisy file starves every *other* file in that root). The scenario that decided it, named by the sponsor as realistic: an app at debug logging appending to a log inside the repo; the cap costs ~17 git processes per 3 s for that root, and `ignore_globs` (watcher scope only) is the user's lever. Alternative: trailing-edge only. Two-way door (one constant beside the 750 ms one). Scenario B12 added to `01-scenarios.md` by the worker's proposal.
  - **Claude Design pass → Phase 9 kickoff agenda** `[DECIDED]`: the sponsor: the UI "is perfectly usable as-is", the pass "may just be minor polish towards the end of the phases". Risk assessment on record: low — the screen is a function of state, design changes stay in the render module, snapshots regenerate mechanically; the one thing that grows is layout special-casing (header ladder, hint tiers, narrow rule), so Phases 6–8 add no new layout concept without a `docs/dev/tui.md` note naming it as pass input. Alternative: run the pass before the Phase 6 worker. Operational.
  - **Discovery shows a stderr line, not a frame** `[DECIDED]`: `lastcall: discovering roots under <dir>…` printed before `Engine::open`, replaced by the alternate screen; `init_tracing` moves ahead of the open. Alternative (costed by the review: an optional watcher in the loop, Ctrl-C as a key with exit 0, an open-failure exit path, re-derive on open, a backend-generic loop, three tests): a real `discovering…` frame with the terminal taken first — the trigger to revisit is Ctrl-C wanted during discovery or root counts where discovery takes ten seconds or more. Two-way door.
  - **One Opus 5 worker with a sanctioned checkpoint after deliverable 5** `[DECIDED]` (engine half: gate scenarios, D7/D8 at the defaults, `hunks_of`, one remote-ref listing; then the TUI half). Alternative: two workers on one branch. Operational.

One of the things that I'm keeping in mind is that a lot of people will continue moving towards software factories and, um, basically unattended coding where they're not going to see the code until the PR is finished by a software factory. I think that that's still a good thing to do, um, especially you could do a software factory locally that, you know, um, does a whole bunch of work, and then you still can review it as it's going along. So if it was possible for us to have a simple picker of agents, that would probably make the most sense from a capability perspective because we definitely don't want to just send to a random agent. The sponsor's own sketch of the feature, which the items below shape (verbatim): "1. a new option is available to "flag for review" by a shortcut keypress for a hunk (or pressing it when left-nav focus on a file, could flag an entire file (?) 2. if press that option, then there is a way to type the notes and and shift enter will do a new line, but enter will "send" the request to the agent / The only problemw ith that is it's a one-shot, so if you had multiple things uyou wanted to queue, the feature wouldn't be for that. but we could start with one-shot.... or maybe there is a way to continually send the text concatenated one after the other to the other agent window and then the user has to navigate to the pane and presss Enter on that agent window text box to actually submit the compiled notes?" A deliberate, confirmed exception to invariant 3 (the editor session is the review). Sponsor's question that shaped it: "do we have a guard to tell if the file hashed content changed and if so, warn? … so that at least someone knows, and they can abort?" — the answer: the pre-launch CAS refuses to open a moved file, the editor's own changed-on-disk warning guards the session, and the confirm guards the return.   - **Worker #2 needs-a-ruling 1 — signals without `SIG_IGN` — taken (trust-me item, recorded for the sponsor)**: the kickoff's deliverable 7 told the worker to ignore `SIGINT`/`SIGQUIT` with `SIG_IGN` through nix and restore the child's dispositions in `pre_exec`; both are `unsafe fn` and the workspace forbids unsafe code, so the rule was unimplementable as written. What was built (`run.rs` `Signals`): registering tokio's `SignalKind::quit()` stream installs a process-lifetime handler, so a `^\` typed at the editor cannot core-dump lastcall behind it; on resume the streams are recreated and a latched `SIGINT` is drained for 50 ms (`EDITOR_SETTLE`), so a `^C` typed at the editor cannot quit lastcall when the loop runs again (`pty_editor_ctrl_c_does_not_quit_lastcall`). `exec` resets handlers to the default in the child either way. **Residual, accepted**: a real `ctrl-c` inside that 50 ms window is swallowed and must be pressed again. Alt on record: lift `unsafe_code = "forbid"` to `deny` with one audited `#[allow]` for a `pre_exec` block — rejected for one keystroke's 50 ms window.
  - **Worker #2 needs-a-ruling 2 — `ExportContext.attribution` — moved to the Phase 9 kickoff agenda**: Phase 7's `engine.md` said "Phase 8 provides it"; Phase 8's deliverable list never contained it and nothing built it. Whether the herdr attribution ships in v0.1.0 or stays with the post-v1 attribution layer (§6.8) is a Phase 9 kickoff question (§8 agenda); §11 entry added.

Findings folded as review-named commits (`97a0099..4e6a358`): F1 MINOR (macOS: a key typed while the editor runs, or in the ~50 ms settle window, sits unread until the next key — crossterm's kqueue source is edge-triggered and the raw-mode switch moves the pending line without a wakeup; fixed by a fire-and-forget cursor-position request after `term::enter()` whose reply wakes the queue and is consumed internally; the PTY harness answers the request behind a switch so a scene proves it; residual on a silent terminal documented); F2 MINOR (a second `i` in flight replaced a dirty inline buffer — refused while an editor is open, stale answers dropped by generation); F3 MINOR (the `ctrl-c` scene now ends with a real `^C` so `tui.md`'s claim is what it runs); F4 NIT (`not found: <program>` leads with the verb); F6 NIT (the OSC 52 scene decodes the payload test-side instead of comparing against the encoder under test); F7 NIT (the tier-3 hint needs 128 columns on a file row and 121 on a root row — both recorded and pinned). Recorded, no change: F5 (an external `kill -QUIT` is swallowed for the process lifetime by the registered quit stream — the accepted price of no `SIG_IGN`; §11), F8 (`LASTCALL_KEYBOARD` and the `$VISUAL`/`$EDITOR` closure read the environment outside `Env` — documented, pure-closure shape).
  - **Gate 8 sponsor run (2026-09-06, live, release build in a herdr pane, this repo as the root) — four observations, three fixes on the branch, the rest passed:** (i) the `$EDITOR`-return confirm `<path> changed while your editor was open — mark as reviewed?` fired after the sponsor's own ordinary save and read as "someone else touched this" (sponsor: "that's very misleading UX"). lastcall is suspended for the whole session and only knows the bytes differ on return, so the wording claimed a detection it cannot make. **Reworded, mechanism unchanged** (`cd2006f`): `<path> edited — mark every hunk in it reviewed?` — the sponsor's framing, verbatim in substance: "because we know that they edited the whole file … we could ask them, do you want to accept all hunks or leave them … that would make it a bit more honest"; Enter blesses, Esc leaves every hunk pending; no count shown because the row on screen may predate the save. Ruling P1 + confirm and Amendment v1.8 stand as frozen; the quoted string in the P1 bullet above and in the kickoff is superseded by this one. Alt considered and not taken (orchestrator's first Rec, withdrawn after the sponsor's proposal): no confirm and no blessing on return — re-render and `a`. (ii) after an inline save on the last pending file the selection wrapped to a far-away row ("spat me onto a random file"): `App::advance` selects the next row by path in the root, else the root's first remaining row, else the next root's first row — deterministic, but a teleport. Sponsor's ask: neighbour-first (the row below, else above) and something clean when the root is empty. Touches §6.7's frozen wording → **Phase 9 kickoff agenda** (§8), not changed here. (iii) **Launch flash, root-caused and fixed (`66e09ac`)**: the sponsor screen-recorded the first ~2 s of a launch from a herdr pane over 20 roots — the badge went `standalone` → `herdr 0.8.2`, the left pane showed one root, went blank behind `nothing pending across 20 roots`, showed a different root, then all eight listed. A debug-logged relaunch (`LASTCALL_LOG=debug`) showed the scope going `None → {one root} → {another} → None` while nothing about the workspace changed. Cause: herdr reports every pane's `foreground_cwd` from the process table, and in the pane lastcall runs in the foreground is lastcall and its `git` children — so during the initial scans our own pane "was in" whichever root a `git` was running in, `pane_updated` carried it, and the §6.6 containment fallback (the sponsor's workspace has no worktree provenance) narrowed the scope to that root, hiding everything else. Three fixes, all two-way doors under the delegation: (1) `HERDR_PANE_ID` is read beside `HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID` and our own pane's `foreground_cwd` is scrubbed before any derivation (`herdr::own_pane_scrubbed`; its shell `cwd` still counts) — our own subprocesses are never evidence about the workspace; (2) `HerdrView::scope_pending` holds every root unlisted from launch until the first scope verdict (a derived scope, including `None`; a standalone start; a failed connect; a link that drops before its snapshot), so the first pile can no longer be listed and then hidden — the right pane says `waiting for herdr scope…` for that interval instead of `nothing pending`; (3) the empty state under an active scope now reads `nothing pending in <label>` with the in-scope roots and `N repos hidden (w shows all)` — `nothing pending across 20 roots` was untrue while the scope hid eight with piles. Not changed: another pane's `foreground_cwd` still moves the scope (a `cd` in a sibling shell is real evidence under §6.6) — its jitter is a **Phase 9 kickoff agenda** item (debounce or shell-cwd-only). Open item, §11: the sponsor's launch showed this repo with nothing pending while the orchestrator's scans of the same state dir (`~/.local/state/lastcall`, same `HOME`) show it with pending rows from the 2026-09-04 ledger — unexplained; to be reproduced at the Phase 9 kickoff before anything is changed. (v) **Launch hold (2026-09-07, `9d2fd4a`)**: after (iii) the sponsor's re-test still showed one root (BMAD-METHOD) listed for ~100 ms before the full list — the engine's deliberate solo first-root scan (Phase 5 deliverable 1b) landing its pile before the batch. The orchestrator presented it as a taste call with the numbers; the sponsor: "honestly a better UX/UI would be 'discovered N roots, checking status….' … rather than show a single repo and flash it making it seem that it's the only repo with changes while loading everything else", then asked for progress "if people really have crazy stuff going on in their repos … a counter which increments on number of repos scanned, objects scanned" shown only "when something is going to take longer than 1 second … where flashing meaningly 'loading... 23423423432' has no user value". Options presented (per-repo count, files-so-far, elapsed; honest limits: no intra-repo progress, files only after a repo's scan returns); verdict verbatim: **"1 rec is great because people can see if a single repo is holding things up (e.g. hundreds of thousands of files in that 1 root)" / "2 rec"**. Built (two-way door under the delegation): `App::Loading` holds every root unlisted from `sync_roots` until each has reported (`EngineEvent::Scanned` — new, progress-only, `try_send` from the pool thread through `Engine::scan_all_with` — or its pile or a `scan failed` notice; a global notice ends the hold); the pane reads `discovered N roots, checking status…` with a line per root, and from one second (`Loading::COUNTER_AFTER`) adds `K of N repos checked · F files pending so far · Ss` and a `✓` per reported root; the solo first-root scan is folded into the batch (`watcher::run_loop`), so `bench.md` S1 `first_pile_ms` now floors at ~1 s and `first_frame_ms` is the comparable number. Not built: intra-repo progress (the time is inside `git`). Design-pass row added (§8). **Sponsor re-test 2026-09-07, approved:** the rebuilt release binary from the same pane — "looked perfect - just a brief status before it loaded"; then a stretched load through a PATH `git` shim (`z_ignore/slowgit`, sleeps before the scan-only `diff-files`/`ls-files` calls; one repo made slower) to see the ~10 s form — "absolutely excellent UX/UI behavior … if someone has a massive set of repos/files, it will be very clear what's happening — no notes … sponsor approved". The Gate 8 sponsor run is closed. The shim is committed as `scripts/slowgit/git` with `just probe-tui-slow` (sponsor: "a nice thing to commit permanently … how it helps validate UX/UI at scale or on slower hardware"; `tui.md` "Seeing the hold slowly", `testing.md` "The slow git"). The shim's first catch: the TUI read its root list through the engine lock after the watcher had started, and under a stretched scan the watcher's initial `scan_all` won the lock, so the `discovering roots…` line stood for the whole scan and no hold was drawn — fixed by reading the roots before the watcher exists (`4b51d52`; a fast checkout had hidden it). (iv) **Passed as written**: steps 0–5 of the sponsor sheet — `$EDITOR` at the line with the reworded confirm, the inline editor's band and `^S`, select-to-copy with `v`/`y` and with a mouse drag (herdr's terminal; the clipboard received the shown lines), the note modal's Shift-Enter newline, arrow keys and alt-backspace, and the whole-file flag export with its summary line; the keyboard-enhancement probe showed no visible launch difference against `LASTCALL_KEYBOARD=plain`.
  - **Sponsor items carried on PR #8** (not gate items — design review F14): OSC 52 arrival in a real clipboard is write-only and unprovable from a test — the sponsor tries `y` in herdr's terminal, iTerm2 and tmux (`set-clipboard on`); Terminal.app does not implement OSC 52 and shift+drag stays the path there. The sponsor times one herdr launch against the ≤2 s keyboard probe (P9). Merging the PR ratifies Amendment v1.8.

- **2026-09-07 — Phase 9 kickoff: the split, the rulings and the ledger discrepancy** `[DECIDED]` (`98-phase9a-kickoff.md` frozen; `99-phase9b-release-kickoff.md` parked). The full round (P1–P13, design-pass D1–D25, ledger review L1–L22, 27 items) was too much for the sponsor ("this is actually worse because you've taken a lot of complexity and boiled it down into even more dense language that I don't know what I'm agreeing to … can you only give me the things that are explicitly about code that we need to do to fix current things and then give me the normal details"); the round was re-cut to thirteen code/UX items with scenarios, and the release, docs and process items were held back. Verdicts, verbatim: **2** — a new product behaviour, the sponsor's: "always display the git repo … even if there's no changes staged within it. So if I have ten or twenty git clones that I'm seeing because I'm in the parent directory, then I'll be able to see all of them … when I accept the last file, the cursor goes to highlight the repo name … add a simple keyboard toggle to hide repos that have nothing in them … by default you would always see every repo"; on the design put back to him (dim name-and-branch row, `nothing pending in <name>`, `t`, `hide_empty_repos = false`, independent of the herdr scope): "that design is great" — **replaces the neighbour-first rule (P5)** and amends §6.7 (Amendment v1.9); **3** focus-true tier 0, `v select` kept — "agreed with the 'makes sense' approach"; **4** the sponsor's own hint rule — "should we consider just trying our best to fit everything but once it gets beyond N columns, cut it back and add `? help`" (no width thresholds; drop one at a time from the right; `? help  q quit` pinned) — replaces D1; **5, 6, 7** rec (hold vocabulary "repos" + one clock + header agrees; ✓ column — "much cleaner"; scope wait folded into the hold frame — "fewer flashes"); **9** copy cue kept as built — "matches the normal way herdr works, not a problem" (D8 rejected); **10** "agreed - shorten the left side as proposed"; **12** "agreed that makes sense"; **13** closed, see below. **Parked** by the sponsor as the §3.5 circle-back "herdr scope: is it over-complicated?": **1** (sibling-pane jitter — "the description is not clear to me on what this scenario is", then "I think we may have made the product too complex - let's circle back on this"), **8** (the scope notice — "I don't get why a herdr workspace in dev/git/myrepo with lastcall invoked in dev/git/myrepo would show '19 repos'"; answer on record: only a launch from the *parent* directory inside a one-repo workspace hides anything), **11** (the status-line age — "why do we need the time since message? what am I forgetting about our product design here?"; answer: nothing — it was a Phase 7 design-pass defensive touch, not a requirement). Sponsor on parking: "if my brain isn't getting some of these, it's probably better to just push them to another time … I don't want to dishonor them or leave good UX/UI decisions unanswered, so I'd rather wait to give them more time"; then "agreed to pause the circle back ones and proceed with the clean, agreed items for now in a new kickoff". **The §11 ledger discrepancy, explained (agenda (k), deliverable 0):** every sponsor run used `LASTCALL_STATE_DIR=$(mktemp -d)` (his pasted invocation from `~/dev/git`, 2026-09-07 10:33: `LASTCALL_STATE_DIR=$(mktemp -d) LASTCALL_LOG_FILE=/tmp/lastcall-gate5.log LASTCALL_LOG=debug ~/dev/git/lastcall/target/release/lastcall` — the prefix came from the orchestrator's Gate 5 sponsor sheet), so each run wrote to a fresh store under `$TMPDIR` (`/var/folders/…/T/tmp.*`; nine found, read-only: the Gate 8 run's `tmp.JnOT1abpiK` 2026-09-06 18:17–19:21 with this repo's ledger re-stamped 19:21:35, and `tmp.wGg0lkvPmK` 20:15–21:16), never to `~/.local/state/lastcall`; the `state_dir` the §11 entry cited was the orchestrator's. No lastcall bug; no migration (those trees have moved on; macOS purges the temp dirs); the hardening (`status` names its store) ships as 9a deliverable 1; the §11 entry is closed. Also decided: 9a runs as one Opus 5 worker with a sanctioned checkpoint after the nav change; `t` is the toggle key (free in `DEFAULT_KEYMAP`); the branch is `feat/phase9a-behaviour`. - **2026-09-11 — the perf gate's targets, per scenario, and run G** `[DECIDED]`. Gate 9's perf clause is discharged by a **target per scenario**, set here against the Phase 4 baseline (run A) and the phase runs since, and evidenced by **run G**: two `just bench` runs back to back on one quiet machine, 2026-09-11, `5 passed; 0 failed` each (`docs/dev/bench.md`, "Phase 9b (run G)"). Baseline → target → run G, by scenario: **S1** (100 roots, 4,000 rows) `open_ms` 33,050 → ≤ 6,000 → 3,704 / 3,641; `scan_all_ms` 25,553 → ≤ 6,000 → 4,085 / 4,018; `first_frame_ms` 56,568 → ≤ 12,000 → 7,339 / 7,308. **S1h** (50 roots, 80 files each) `first_frame_ms` no Phase 4 row, Phase 5 run C 4,782 → ≤ 6,500 → 3,930 / 4,064. **S2** (one 100,000-line file) `scan_ms` 270 → ≤ 350 → 211 / 209. **S3** (1,000 files under watch) `settle_ms` 1,553 → ≤ 2,000 → 1,522 / 1,508, and **S3_events** 1,553 → ≤ 2,000 → 1,513 / 1,543. **S4** (50,000 files, the row cap) `capped_count_ms` 3,617 → ≤ 5,500 → 3,690 / 3,795; `settle_ms` 5,342 → ≤ 7,000 → 5,314 / 5,461. Every row is under its target in both runs. The targets are the busy-machine runs D–F plus 20–35 % headroom, not run A × 0.8: after Phase 5's pool the literal reading of "no scenario regresses more than 20 % against its Phase 4 baseline" would be met by a build six times slower than this one, so the gate becomes a ratchet against the numbers we actually ship (ruling P7; the Alt, a floor against catastrophe, was not taken). Spawn counts are asserted **equal**, not bounded: S1 1,102 / 1,600, S1h 552 / 800, S2 16, S4 15, and run G reproduced every one of them bit-identically, as runs D, E and F did. Run G also settles what runs E and F could only predict: every wall time they reported as up by 10–28 % is back at run D's level, so that drift was machine load, not code. Two numbers are recorded as noisy rather than averaged: S1h `scan_all_ms` (3,728 vs 3,143 between the two runs; not a gate row) and S2 `peak_rss_kb` (32.8 %; the peak is sampled on a timer). One harness fix was needed to complete the run — the `first_checked_ms` wait (renamed from `first_pile_ms` here, forward only) waited for the launch hold's one-second counter, which a quiet machine's warm scan never reaches; it now also accepts the hold being over, which is the same fact stated more strongly, and on a quiet run `first_checked_ms` equals `first_frame_ms`. That wait was written with the hold at `9d2fd4a` (2026-09-07) and no bench had run it: run F was taken the day before. *(Drafted by the worker in `99-phase9b-release-kickoff.md`; adopted by the orchestrator at the docs commit.)*

- **2026-09-14 — the sponsor's bug arrives (deliverable 4) and becomes Phase 11; deliverable 6 built; the help-overlay regression and its fix; the DX workflows Phase 11 protects** `[DECIDED]`. **The bug, transcribed from speech and paraphrased:** on a repository checked out on a branch he called "my future", an agent added 32 files, all pure additions; he accepted the hunks and files in lastcall as they came; then, in his words, "I checked out main, and then the files showed as now deleted", thirty-two files with the hunks now deleted, and "I had to reaccept every single file or just reaccept the whole repo, but it wasn't a very good user experience." The orchestrator's reading from the model: not a defect in the accept path but the seen-tree model's designed branch-switch over-show (§6.4: HEAD is never consulted, the baseline is one private seen tree plus overrides, and a switch shows `switched X → Y: N files differ from seen state`); his accepts on the branch became overrides holding the added content, and `main`'s working tree, which has none of it, differs from that seen state by 32 deletions. The deferral ledger already names the hardening ("No per-branch seen trees", trigger "in-place branch hopping becoming a daily pattern"); the trigger has fired. Rec/Alt: 1 whether it is a Phase 10 fix or its own mini-phase (Rec: own mini-phase after this PR merges, the change is engine-deep and Phase 10 is verified; Alt: hold the PR); 2 the shape (Rec: per-branch seen trees keyed by the checked-out branch; Alt: a smarter switch notice only). **The sponsor, verbatim: "1 rec 2 rec --- but let's walkt hrough it. say that the agent had checked out a feat/my-feat-1 and did the 32 files, then committed, then checked out main again..... what is shown in lastcall if I never accpeted anything?"** Answer given: nothing pending, because `main`'s working tree equals the seen tree; the deletions appear only for what he accepted while on the branch. He then described the agent workflows the product has to protect (paraphrased, and kept generic on his instruction): an unattended run cuts a branch with a generated name from the current HEAD, works and commits on it, discards its uncommitted changes (`git checkout .` and `git clean -fd`), checks the start branch back out and leaves the branch in place; several such runs go back to back; later their commits are **cherry-picked** onto a named feature branch, so every commit gets a new id and the run branch is never an ancestor of the feature branch; a variant does the same in a linked worktree. He wants to review and mark seen at several points in that flow. Orchestrator's reading: the residual pending work he expected after the discard is the same bug (accepts made while the run branch was checked out, then its content gone from the working tree), so per-branch trees close it and the workflow needs no change; lastcall's state lives outside the repository so `git clean -fd` cannot touch it; the cherry-pick means "seen" must follow blob content (identical content keeps its blob id across a cherry-pick) and never commit ancestry, which the model already forbids; and the run branches persisting makes reviewing a branch without checking it out viable. **Phase 11 is therefore framed as more combinations of protective behaviour for the DX workflows lastcall sits beside, each written as a frozen scenario (D12 onward) with its harness and integration-test forms before the build.** **Phase 11 shape, ruled the same day.** The orchestrator, asked which pieces were least surprise and most valuable, put two items: (1) per-branch seen trees and nothing more (Rec), a branch's record starting as a copy of the one just left so the switch itself shows nothing new, cherry-picked content showing once more on the feature branch by design because hiding work is the one surprise the product must never spring, with the `feat/x: N unseen` row note as the Alt; (2) discovery one folder into a plain directory (Rec) against documenting a second `parent_dirs` entry (Alt). **The sponsor: `1 rec`, and for 2 a better shape than either: say it in the first-launch tour.** A third choice card, `Where lastcall looks`, of the same form as the herdr and empty-repository cards: the list holds every repository directly inside the parent directory; a repository one folder deeper, such as `worktrees/<name>`, is not listed unless asked; `Keep looking one folder down` or `Look two folders down, and remember that` (writes `search_depth = 2`); deeper than two, or clones and worktrees kept somewhere else entirely, go in the config file as `search_depth = N` and one `parent_dirs` entry per place, the config path printed as the pointer. Depth 1 stays the default; depth N walks plain folders only, skipping the dependency directories the draft walk skips, capped at that walk's ceiling; depth 2 also lists a worktree kept inside a repository (`repo/.worktrees/<name>`) as its own badged row, which gets its own scenario. Sub-ruling 2a, when the card shows: Rec only when a plain folder holds repositories that depth 1 missed, naming the folder and the count; Alt on every first launch as a general lesson. **The sponsor: `2a alt on FTUE`.** The key name `search_depth` is the orchestrator's taste call under the standing delegation. Branch rows without a checkout and seen-follows-content are not in Phase 11.** Deliverable 4 closes as `received 2026-09-14; own mini-phase (Phase 11)`. **Deliverable 6** (worker, Opus 5, `bfcc0fe` reducer + keymap + help, `431d523` PTY `pty_nav_jumps`, `0ac4c55` docs; report `z_ignore/review-phase10/worker-report-d6.md`). Its four decisions, accepted as judgment calls under the delegation (two-way doors): (R-4) `home`/`end` with nothing selected select the ends; (R-5) a repository jump with nothing selected starts at the first repository row; (R-6) `nav_top`/`nav_bottom` move a live `v` selection's far end like the page keys; (R-7) the PTY scene is `pty_nav_jumps` (the file's naming convention, not the kickoff's `tui_nav_jumps`). **The orchestrator's own regression:** the kickoff placed the four help rows right after `nav_page_down`, which pushed `Ctrl-A`, `u`, `U` and `z` under the 80×24 fold, the exact state verifier F3 had repaired; moving them below `refresh` restored that page but put a 29-column description in the right column of the 100-column two-column form, which then needed 103 columns and folded sixteen keys at 100×30. Fixed `4bfbe47`: the rows sit right after `undo`, the descriptions read `first entry / top of diff` and `last entry / end of diff` within the right column's 26-column budget, four help snapshots regenerated, the kickoff amended with a dated note. **Verifier on deliverable 6, `c79fb8f` and `a04adc8`** (fresh context, `z_ignore/review-phase10/verify-d6.md`, eleven pty probes): FIXES REQUIRED, F1 to F7, none touching a ruling; all fixed under the delegation in `cae761e` except F3, which is accepted. **F1** MAJOR: the docs told Terminal.app users to turn on "Use Option as Meta key"; a terminal that sends Meta as an escape prefix delivers Option-Up as `ESC ESC [ A`, which crossterm 0.29 decodes as `Esc`, `[`, `A`, so the "jump" ran `hunk_prev` and then `accept_file` (reproduced: an accepted file). - **2026-09-14 (after the `v0.2.0` release) — Phase 11 kicked off; the design pending its review** `[PENDING the adversarial design review and the sponsor's rulings]`. Branch `feat/phase11-branch-seen` from `509ab58`; kickoff `110-phase11-kickoff.md`. The design as drafted, numbered for the review: (R1) the record in force is the branch named by `<git_dir>/HEAD`, read as a file at every scan and head inspection, so the pile never depends on which watcher event came first; (R2) a branch's first sight is a copy of the record just left, then a fold that takes the new branch's committed content for every path differing between the two tips when the new tip is already in the old branch's history (the return to `main` after accepting on a feature branch, the case in the report, shows nothing; an ahead or diverged branch, the cherry-pick case, keeps the copy and over-shows its committed differences as ruled); (R3) parked records under `branches`, pruned when their ref is gone; (R4) detached, unborn and in-progress states never switch; (R5) a switch is a locked read-modify-write and an accept staged under another branch is refused with a notice; (R6) schema 1.2, additive, the record in force at the top level so a `v0.2.0` binary keeps working on a 1.2 file; (R7) compaction, undo and flags scoped to the record in force; (R8) the first-sight notice text; (R9) two additive `status --json` fields. The headline question for the sponsor is R2's fold against a pure copy (the pure copy re-creates the reported bug whenever the branch returned to was never seen while lastcall watched). Also on this branch, by the sponsor's note the same day about `lastcall update` refusing a cargo-installed binary on a second machine: the README's Install section is rewritten as two routes, prebuilt and cargo, each with its upgrade step and which one `update` serves (kickoff deliverable 6). **Design review 2026-09-15** (fresh context, `z_ignore/review-phase11/design-review-phase11.md`): FIXES REQUIRED, F1 to F14, none touching R1 to R4; every hunt for a hide came back empty and the reviewer's own evaluation of the pure-copy alternatives found that none fixes the reported case (seeding from the new branch's tree hides a never-seen branch's work; dropping overrides equal to the source tree leaves the accepted additions as deletions). Folded the same day under the delegation, all two-way doors: F1 a same-commit first sight keeps `switched A → B (same commit)` and only a moved head gets the first-sight form (B4's asserted string changes, B2's does not); F2 D19's notice and pile stated from the classifier and the content model; F3 the fold keeps flag-only overrides as `ops::fold` does; F4 D15's pile stated before and after the clean; F5 the refusal reaches only an `Ops` built from a stale ledger, adopts the disk ledger and drops its staged work, D22 written to reach it; F6 the sync is truthful about git's worktree-then-`HEAD` window and `commit` re-reads `HEAD` under the lock; F7 an operation that ends on another branch files its mid-way accepts on the starting branch, a stated non-goal; F8 prune by full ref names, existence by `rev-parse --verify`, prune skipped on error, the current branch's rename re-labels the record in force; F9 the sync is a `RootState` step called from `scan_root`, `inspect_head` and the tail of `open_root_with`; F10 `seen_branch` is tri-state on the wire; F11 to F14 citations, harness entry points, a worker-directed clause dropped, the fold's older-history edge named. Open for the sponsor: R2 itself, the copy plus the ancestor fold against the pure copy his ruling worded.

- **2026-09-15 (later) — the sponsor's gate run: the reported case passes, a branch cut from `main` fails; R2 widened to the merge-base** `[DECIDED]`. With the branch binary running over his repositories, the orchestrator cut a branch in one of them and committed three files on it; the sponsor accepted them, checked `main` out and came back: "perfect - I saw 0 files load as I switched branches back and forth". Asked to try a branch cut from `main` next, the orchestrator ran the checkout of `main`, the cut and a one-file commit in one shell line, inside lastcall's debounce window. The sponsor: "hrm... it shows 4 files pending - 3 removals of the one / three / two files plus the new lastcall-check-2 file". The ledger confirmed the cause: lastcall never observed `main`, saw one switch from the first branch to the second, and the two tips had diverged (each one commit past `main`), so the ancestor form of R2 kept the copy, three accepted files and all. An over-show, not a hide, but a screen that depends on whether a second passed between two commands. The sponsor asked how this escaped testing and how a fix can be known not to open a new leak; answered that D19 pinned the diverged case as the designed over-show, so the tests and both reviews faithfully guarded a rule that was too narrow, and that the answer to the second question is a randomized oracle test against the one safety sentence, not another hand-written scenario. Asked what the right screen is, the orchestrator's position: on the new branch you see the one file you added there and nothing else, because a branch's pile is what is in its working tree that you have not looked at, and a screen that depends on lastcall's timing is the one thing a review ledger must not have. Presented as one item: Rec, fold to the merge-base of the two tips (identical to the ruled rule when the new branch is behind or ahead; on a diverged pair the files accepted up to the old tip fold to the common ancestor and the new branch's own commits show), with the oracle property test built alongside and a verifier told to hunt the merge-base form specifically; Alt, keep the ruled rule and record the case as a known over-show cleared by one `A`. The sponsor ruled **"1 rec"**. Writing D25 surfaced a second consequence of the ancestor form, Variant B: arriving on a cherry-picked branch from the run branch whose work had been accepted, the carried override's blob equals the cherry-picked content and the file was hidden, seen-follows-content by accident, against D16; the merge-base form folds the accepted path away and the cherry-pick shows. Spec edits: R2 and its rationale, D25 in both files, kickoff deliverable 7 (the oracle: property 1 no hide, property 2 timing independence, the generator's discipline keeping the §11 residual outside), Amendment v1.12, the §8 gate line reopened for D25 and the oracle, the sponsor box annotated. The push waits for the rule as ruled; the earlier close-out entry stands as the record of the ancestor form.
- **2026-09-16 — the merge-base fold built and verified; the third verifier found it baselining content never on a screen; R2 tightened to the seen-state target** `[DECIDED]`. **Built** by a worker (Opus 5) in four commits on the merge-base amendment: `fe6a4bd` (D25 and its two variants in the D suite and the harness, red first), `ac9f3e0` (the seen oracle, `test_integration_seen_oracle.rs`, red first against the ancestor form: the unwatched run of the D25 shape manufactured `g1:Deleted`, and proptest shrank the same family to six operations on its own), `1306ef5` (`fold_onto_first_sight` targets `git merge-base <refs/heads/A> HEAD`; one process replaces one), `d994368` (docs); the orchestrator's `89b5103` (the harness cherry-pick scenarios pin the pick's committer date: a pick inside the same second as the commit it copies reproduces that commit's sha, which made D25 Variant B flake three runs in six) and `cd7efcb` (deliverable 7 as built). Six judgment calls of that round, ruled by the orchestrator under the sponsor's 2026-09-05 delegation: (1) Property 2 is the subset direction, not the equality, because a branch the watching run passes through gets a record of its own and the watching run then over-shows, which §2 allows (Alt, keep the equality and let every run go red for a reason the fold has no say in); (2) Property 1 cannot be the one that catches D25 Variant B, since `Seen` is keyed by path as the sentence is and the cherry-picked entry is the entry the user accepted elsewhere (Alt, key `Seen` by branch, which fails the legitimate first-sight carry); (3) the generator never deletes the branch whose record is in force, the fail-open row pinned by hand (Alt, weaken the property after every delete); (4) four to seven operations a case, about 2.5 s each, so 64 cases hold near three minutes (Alt, cut the case count, which the delegation's own instrument forbids); (5) D25's notice reads `committed on feat2 (1 commit)` because the head watcher classifies a move by the reflog's top entry and the one shell line ends in a commit, the first-sight wording parked as a circle-back (Alt, let the first sight outrank the reflog class, which reaches B4, several D scenarios and a PTY scene); (6) the pinned committer date in the harness. **Verified** by a third fresh-context verifier told to find a topology where the merge-base fold hides (`z_ignore/review-phase11/verify-mergebase.md`): **fixes required.** F1 MAJOR, confirmed on eight histories: the fold checked that a path was finished on the departed branch and never that the merge-base's entry had been on a screen, so a version committed and reverted while the pile was never accepted, a mode flip, a symlink, an unaccepted deletion, the content of a never-visited branch merged or rebased in and then checked out, a criss-cross whose outcome depended on which of two merge-bases git printed first, and the diverged form of the §11 residual all became seen state on the new branch; six of the eight were hidden by the ancestor form too, and the §11 entry had recorded one member of the family as needing an accept, an intermediate commit and a cut exactly there, none of which it needs. F2 MAJOR, confirmed: the oracle's Property 2 fails on a five-operation shape its own generator produces (a branch cut and never looked at shows the seed's file as added, the watched run shows nothing), a pre-push gate that can go red at random; the merge-base form's second guard refused the fold that would have made the two runs agree. F3 MINOR: the oracle was green over F1's family only because its generator discipline could not build the shapes; lifted, it reports the hide on its own. F4 MINOR: the harness twin writes a directory entry into the index on one shape and re-labels a rename onto a parked name; F5 MINOR: four prose errors, including a CHANGELOG sentence the code contradicted and the kickoff's claim that a first sight restamps `seen_at`; F6 NIT: a fold abandoned on a git failure leaves no notice. Everything else byte-identical against the previous binary (60 of 62 outputs; the two that differ are D25 and the diverged pair, the intended change). The orchestrator reproduced F1's first shape and F2's shape with the verifier's scripts before presenting. **Presented** as one item. Rec: fold a path to the merge-base's entry only when that entry is already seen state, either because the merge-base is reachable from the commit the root was first sighted at (a new additive ledger field, `first_sight_head`, unknown in files made by earlier versions, where only the second form applies) or because some record, in force or parked, already composes that entry as its baseline; the sentence of deliverable 7 applied to the fold itself; closes all eight shapes and the random red gate with one rule; the sponsor's three cases still fold; the cost is an honest over-show where the main line advanced through merges nobody looked at. Alt: keep the merge-base rule, pin the random-red shape as an accepted over-show and rewrite the residual to say that a branch cut at a commit nobody looked at baselines that commit's content, which wins only if a quiet cut from `main` matters more than the sentence. The sponsor ruled **"1 rec"** and asked, in his words, whether this is "forwards, not backwards" on resolving the last bugs; the orchestrator's answer: each round narrowed the same rule toward one sentence, and this round makes the fold ask that question directly instead of approximating it with ancestry, with the oracle now able to build the shapes that would prove otherwise. Under the delegation the orchestrator also ruled: the generator discipline is lifted and the shapes it cannot express are pinned by hand (F3; Alt, keep the discipline and the `before`-blob hardening, which cannot work because `accept_all` compacts overrides into the seen tree); the harness twin gets the seen-state target and its two disagreements fixed toward the engine (F4); a skipped fold gets a notice (F6); the prose corrected (F5).
