The push waits for the rule as ruled, a fresh verification with the same probes, and the sponsor's re-run.
- **2026-09-16 — the seen-state target built and verified; Phase 11 close-out** `[DECIDED]`. **Built** by a worker (Opus 5) in five commits on the seen-state amendment: `bcc8b46` (D26 and its three variants in the D suite and the harness, red first: 7 of 156 harness assertions failing, the four integration legs failing on the merge-base form), `9aa25b6` (the oracle without its generator discipline, with a mode flip as a generator operation and six hand-driven shapes pinned beside the random cases, red first: 6 of 11 failing at 64 cases), `23cf27a` (`fold_onto_first_sight` folds a path only when the composed baseline equals the departed tip's entry and the merge-base's entry is already seen state, through a persisted `first_sight_head` or a record that composes exactly that entry; a fold that cannot run leaves the copy and a reason), `720da4f` (the harness twin, and its two disagreements moved toward the engine), `161de6d` (`docs/dev/engine.md`); the orchestrator's `d52ab85` (the harness count in `01-scenarios.md`). Counts after: unit 733 = 340 + 20 + 336 + 37, integration 122, harness 156, prepush 130 with the oracle near three minutes, PTY and snapshots unchanged, real-herdr subset green; the orchestrator re-ran every tier and every probe of the third verifier at `161de6d`: all eight histories of its F1 show the path, its F2 shape agrees in both runs, D25 unchanged, the fail-open ladder prints the skipped-fold notice on the two rows that skip. Thirteen judgment calls of the build, ruled by the orchestrator under the sponsor's 2026-09-05 delegation: (1) `first_sight_head` is a top-level field of the 1.2 file, absent when unknown, so older files read and rewrite unchanged and an older binary drops it as it drops the other records (Alt, schema 1.3, which teaches every reader a version for a field whose absence is already the safe answer); (2) an unborn or draft root records no first-sight commit and is never back-filled, so the field means one thing (Alt, take the first commit that appears, which needs a rule for which commit wins); (3) the first-sight clause is reachability, so the first-sight commit is itself seen state (Alt, strict ancestry, which refuses the seed's own entry and reopens D26 Variant C); (4) the record clause asks every record through one composer, minus the record just parked, which provably cannot match (Alt, ask the record in force only, which over-shows an entry another branch plainly holds); (5) a parked seen tree that will not read is dropped from the record clause and the fold goes on, which can only over-show (Alt, abandon the fold, one unreadable object becoming a screen of seen files); (6) a skipped fold is said both in the transition line and once in the root's notices, because a one-shot `status` never inspects a head (Alt, the transition line alone, which is what F6 was raised about); (7) an unknown first-sight commit is not a skip (Alt, a notice on every first switch from an older file, noise on a working path); (8) no git handle is no reason (Alt, a reason string surfacing in draft-root paths); (9) an empty differing set, or a fold allowed to write nothing, is not a skip, since the refusals are already rows on the pile (Alt, "nothing folded" on the most common switch there is); (10) refusal is per path (Alt, all or nothing, one reverted file costing a screen of seen work); (11) the harness twin moved to the engine on both disagreements, a directory at the commit being read is no entry, a checkout of a branch with a parked record is a switch and never a re-label (Alt, move the engine, writing a tree entry where a blob belongs); (12) `status --json` does not report the first-sight commit (Alt, a report-surface change nobody asked for); (13) the CHANGELOG's Unreleased sentence stands as written, since it promised this rule and the merge-base form was what broke the promise (Alt, restate the rule, describing an implementation to someone installing a binary). **Verified** by a fourth fresh-context verifier told to break the seen-state target (`z_ignore/review-phase11/verify-seen-target.md`): **fixes required.** F1 MAJOR, confirmed on two routes: the composer answered "absent" for a parked record with no seen tree and no override for the path, so the record clause took a record that had seen nothing as evidence that every absence had been on a screen, and a deletion committed on the branch left and never accepted folded away on a branch cut at the deleting commit; the routes are a first-sight tree pruned from the object store (an amend, an expired reflog, a `gc`) and an unreadable ledger reopened with nothing seen, each followed by one switch; the harness twin read the record the same way. F2 MINOR: parked records written by the merge-base builds carry baselines that were never on a screen, and this build trusts them through the record clause; only state files from the unpushed builds are affected, which is the sponsor's own machine and nothing published. F3 MINOR: the head-inspection line drops the skipped-fold reason when git's last reflog entry classifies the move as a commit, while the root's notices keep it. F4 NIT: the engine doc promised a notice for a deleted branch that R3's re-label never prints. F5 NIT: R2's "in force or parked" was wider than the code by a provably empty set. F6 NIT: the pre-existing unreadable-ledger notice prints the state path. Everything else held: all eight histories of round three show the path, the random-red shape agrees in both runs, harness and engine agree on every shape tried including four new override shapes, the oracle green at 64, 64 and 200 cases and red on its six pinned shapes under the merge-base engine, nothing else moved between the two binaries, every fail-open row keeps the copy, and every over-show the §11 entry admits clears with one accept. **Fixed** by the orchestrator (`a2fb198`, `9fbd37e`, `194801a`), reproduced first with the verifier's scripts: the composer is three-valued (an entry, absent, or nothing to say), a treeless parked record composes only through an override that names the path, the harness twin says the same, D26 gains Variant D in the harness (156 → 163 assertions, red on the old composer) and the unit test `ops_switch_branch_refuses_the_fold_when_a_parked_record_has_seen_nothing` in the engine (red under the old reading); both of F1's routes show the deletion against the rebuilt binary. Five more judgment calls under the delegation: (14) the treeless record keeps its overrides in the record clause rather than being dropped from the list, because an override is an entry the user accepted (Alt, drop the record whole, simpler and over-showing an accepted entry); (15) Variant D's engine form is a unit test on the fold, since the integration suite has no way to lose a tree (Alt, an integration leg that corrupts the ledger by hand); (16) F3 stays the wording circle-back already parked in the 2026-09-16 entry, the engine doc now saying which surface always carries the reason (Alt, put the reason on the commit-classified line too, which reaches B4 and a PTY scene); (17) F2 is answered by clearing the sponsor's state for the one repository he gate-ran rather than by a schema bump that drops parked records written before `first_sight_head`, which would also drop sound records from roots first sighted at an unborn head (Alt, the bump); (18) F6 rejected: that notice names where the moved-aside file went so the user can recover it, which is its purpose, and it predates this phase. **The sponsor's re-run** on his own repository with the branch binary, after clearing that repository's state from the earlier builds (2026-09-17), three sequences: the reported case (accept on a feature branch, `main` shows nothing deleted, back shows nothing pending), the branch cut from `main` and committed to in one shell line (only the new file pending), and D26's main case (a version committed and put back without an accept, then a branch cut at the middle commit, which showed the file as modified). His words: "A and B passed", and of the third, "Yes that all worked flawlessly."
- **2026-09-17 — `v0.3.0` released; the install routes re-ordered** `[DECIDED]`. Phase 11 shipped as `v0.3.0` (PR #27 the phase, #28 the close-out, #29 the release; the sponsor ruled "1 rec, 2 rec": a minor bump, because behaviour and the ledger schema changed, not only a fix; and the release as its own five-file PR). Using the release, the sponsor found the `cargo install --git … --tag … --force` upgrade line verbose and asked whether that is the community norm. It is not: the norm is a crates.io publish, which shortens the line to `cargo install lastcall`. Presented as one Rec/Alt: Rec, publish on crates.io as a small phase (a one-way door: a published version is never deleted, the name must be free, the engine crate has to be published or folded in); Alt, keep the workspace unpublished and make the prebuilt binary with `lastcall update` the recommended route in the README and `docs/install.md`, with cargo from git as the second option. **Ruled "1 alt for now"**: the update command is the primitive he wants, cargo from git stays secondary. crates.io publishing stays open as a circle-back (with `cargo binstall` and the Homebrew tap of §4.5), two-way until taken.

- **2026-09-17 (later) — draft roots: scope, size, discovery and names; Phase 12 opened** `[DECIDED]`. The sponsor asked how to review files kept in an ignored folder of a repository, was told to list the folder in `draft_dirs`, did so over folders that had grown large, and reported the tool slow and two same-named rows that said nothing about where they live. The orchestrator traced the cost to two places: a draft root's first sight walks, hashes and copies every file below the folder into the private store, and every scan lists them all; and the glob walk reads four levels of every repository on each 30 s backstop tick. **The four rulings**, the sponsor's words paraphrased: (1) a plain `draft_dirs` entry should read the files in that folder only, so a subfolder is reviewed by naming it (`z_ignore/research`), more manual in the config and less of a surprise on disk; the `/**` suffix keeps the recursive behaviour for whoever wants it. (2) Files at or above the size lastcall already collapses (`collapse_size_bytes`, 512 KiB) are not worth reading in a scratch folder at all: skip them, never hash them, say how many in one line. The sponsor asked whether the threshold itself should be smaller in general; the orchestrator's answer was that the collapse threshold is display-only for tracked files and a tracked large file that changed is still a change, so the skip is a draft-root rule and the key stays where it is. (3) The discovery walk should not re-read a large tree every 30 s; the ruling's words were "honour `search_depth`", and the kickoff records the orchestrator's reading (the walk goes as deep as the entry has components, so `z_ignore/research` stays reachable at the default depth) as a judgment call for the close. (4) Names: a new key `draft_dir_parents`, default `1`, prefixes the matched entry with that many directory names above the match point (`repo1/z_ignore`, `repo1/z_ignore/research`; `0` is the entry alone; a folder directly under the parent dir is `git/notes`, not special-cased), superseding the earlier idea of a `[nested in repo1]` badge for drafts, which said nothing for a folder outside any repository; and every selected root shows its full path in the right pane. The sponsor's one worry (a bare `research` entry showing `z_ignore/research` for a folder that lives at `repo1/z_ignore/research`) was confirmed impossible, since a bare entry only matches directly under a base. Behaviour of an existing key changes, so the phase ships as `v0.4.0` with a changelog entry and a one-time trim of records written under the old scope. Kickoff `120-phase12-kickoff.md`; Amendment v1.13 PROPOSED; the adversarial design review runs before a builder starts and its fold is recorded here.
- **2026-09-17 (later still) — Phase 12 design review folded** `[DECIDED]`. The adversarial design review of the kickoff at `cd78be0` (report kept outside the repository) returned FIXES REQUIRED: F1 to F5, with F6 to F11 minor and F12 to F14 text. Every finding is fixed in the kickoff, §6.1, §6.2, §8 and `01-scenarios.md` F4 to F9; none rejected. **Fixed:** F1 (BLOCKER, confirmed by the kickoff's own F6: the size rule as frozen dropped the row of a recorded file that grew past the threshold, a hide; the rule now decides what is *read*, not what is *listed*: a recorded large file that changed is a collapsed unread row until accepted, never-recorded ones stay count-only as ruled). F2 (an enclosing root excluded a plain inner root's whole directory, leaving the files below the inner folder reviewed by nobody; it now excludes exactly what the inner root reads, and F8's `research/deep/c.md` belongs to the outer root). F3 (the kickoff claimed the scan already `lstat`s each draft candidate; it does not; an `lstat` pass before the row cap is added and its cost stated). F4 (a name that depended on the order of entries; the name is taken from the deepest base containing the directory, and matches for one directory are merged). F5 (the trim was gated on open and used a size the engine cannot know; it is by path shape, at any scan). F6 (`--directory` without `--no-empty-directory`, the `.git` check in `Index::others`, the trim named as what bounds the listing). F7 (`Ops::trim` with its side effects named; flag-only overrides on trimmed paths are kept rather than deleted, the orchestrator's choice between the reviewer's two). F8 (the home canonicalized at open; the fixture home as a parameter so the golden test's `HOME` never lands in the OS temp dir; frames asserted free of temp paths). F9 (`literal_separator(true)`, a bare `**` rejected, a Changed entry for the `*` forms). F10 (pile notices carry no root prefix). F12 (five citations corrected; `just snapshots-update` and `just golden-update` named). F13 (long nav names head-ellipsized). F14 (this phase's text says "the sponsor's", and a neighbouring checkout is no longer named in the kickoff). **Ruled by the orchestrator under the sponsor's 2026-09-05 delegation, told to the sponsor at the fold:** F11, the hunk ladder collapses a tracked file at strictly above `collapse_size_bytes` while `config.md` has promised at-or-above: fixed to at-or-above this phase, one character with a Fixed entry (Alt: document the difference and leave the ladder). And F1's consequence for the sponsor's expectation: a seen file that grows large shows one unread row instead of vanishing (Alt: the frozen text, rejected as a hide). **Found by the fold itself:** no config reload exists, so a scope change takes effect at the next start and F7 is a restart scenario; the kickoff's "press `r`" was wrong and is gone.

## 11. Deferral ledger

(standing; entries: deferral, why acceptable now, hardening shape, trigger condition, decided-where)

Standing entries, confirmed at G0 on 2026-09-01 (§10):
- **Event-loss blindness accepted** (no wire sequence numbers): acceptable because invariant 9's resync heals it within 30 s; hardening = shorter fallback or a herdr upstream feature request; trigger = any observed stale-state report in real use.
- **herdr `done` granularity is per-tab, not per-pane:** acceptable at the design workload (one agent per tab in practice); trigger = multi-agent-per-tab workflows becoming common.
- **TUI refresh piles and watcher piles arrive on two channels** (Phase 3 close-out, 2026-09-02): an `r` refresh scans off the UI task and sends its piles on a local channel while the watcher may publish a newer pile for the same root, so a stale pile can win for at most one scan interval; acceptable because the next watcher scan (debounce or the 30 s backstop) heals it and no accept decision is made from the screen in Phase 3; hardening = a scan sequence number on `EngineEvent::Pile`/`Local::Pile` with the reducer dropping older, or routing `r` through the watcher as a rescan request; trigger = a stale pile after `r` observed in use or by a PTY scene, or Phase 4's accept path needing single-channel ordering; decided in §10 "Phase 3 close-out".
- **Per-root row cap (cutoff) not yet designed** (performance amendment, 2026-09-02): until Phase 4 a root with tens of thousands of pending files is scanned in full; acceptable because no such root exists in the design workload and the scan is bounded by git's own index walk; hardening = the cap and its notice, ruled at the Phase 4 kickoff and exercised by the Phase 4 bench's 50,000-file scenario; trigger = that scenario, or any user report of a scan taking longer than 10 s; decided in §10 "Performance gate amendment".
- **Externally created worktrees discovered only by rescan** (herdr emits no events for them, §5.8): acceptable because the periodic rescan bounds staleness to its interval; hardening = shorter interval or watching repo `.git/worktrees/` dirs; trigger = agent-created worktrees routinely missed during live review.
- **Restore's residual hash-then-rename window** (§6.3; review F7): acceptable because the window is microseconds, the post-restore rescan surfaces any delta that lands in it as pending (fail-open), and true cross-process locking against arbitrary agents is not available; hardening = advisory lock honored by cooperating tools, or a restore that writes only when the file's mtime+size are unchanged across the rename; trigger = any observed lost agent write attributed to a restore.
- **Alternates read-miss** (§6.1): our private store references unchanged tracked blobs in the user's repo via `objects/info/alternates`; an object that exists in the user's repo only as an unreachable loose object could be pruned by their `git gc`, after which our reference fails open (that path re-flags in full). Acceptable because objects reachable from any of their refs are never pruned and everything else we wrote ourselves; hardening = copy-on-reference for objects not reachable from HEAD; trigger = any observed spurious re-flag traced to a missing object.
- **Upstream annotation is a heuristic** (§6.4): "reachable from a remote-tracking ref" misclassifies a branch an agent pushed from *another* machine as upstream — it is grouped, not hidden, so the cost is one un-group click; hardening = also require author ≠ `user.email` or a configurable remote allowlist; trigger = the group row routinely containing agent work.
- **No per-branch seen trees** (§6.4): hopping to a never-reviewed branch over-shows its whole delta until you hop back; acceptable at the design workload (agents work in worktrees; in-place hops are brief); hardening = `seen_tree_by_branch` map in the ledger (additive schema change); trigger = in-place branch hopping becoming a daily pattern. **Trigger fired 2026-09-14** (the sponsor's report, §10 that day): hardening built as Phase 11 (`110-phase11-kickoff.md`, Amendment v1.12, the map named `branches` keyed by branch name with the record in force kept at the top level); the entry closes when the merge ratifies the amendment, leaving one residual to record then: a 1.1 binary's first write drops the parked records (over-show at the next switch, never a hide).
- **A branch cut at an older accepted state of a branch that has since re-accepted the path shows the difference** (§6.4, Amendment v1.12, recorded 2026-09-16 from the third Phase 11 verifier's F1, replacing the 2026-09-15 entry "Intermediate committed versions behind an accepted baseline fold", whose whole family the seen-state target closed): the fold takes the merge-base's entry only when it is already seen state, which the ledger knows as the first-sight head's history and the records' current baselines; a version accepted earlier and since replaced is in neither, so a branch cut there shows it as a modification once; a root whose state file predates `first_sight_head` folds through the records alone; acceptable because both are over-shows cleared by one accept; hardening = consulting the record's undo stack, whose entries hold earlier accepted baselines; trigger = a report of accepted content re-shown on a branch cut from an older commit.
- ~~**v0.8.2 lifecycle-subscription replay on connect**~~ (Amendment v1.1): every connect/reconnect replays up to 512 stale lifecycle events over ~50 s, costing coalesced extra snapshots and short-lived `pane_not_found` status subscriptions; acceptable because events are hints and the cost is bounded; hardening = none needed once the pinned release includes herdr's cursor-snapshot fix (master `5158ada`); trigger = a pinned release ≥ that fix (drop the entry) or observed connect-time sluggishness. **Closed 2026-09-11 (Phase 9b, deliverable 14):** the pin moved to v0.9.0, which contains `5158ada` (`gh api repos/herdrdev/herdr/compare/5158ada...v0.9.0` = ahead 55, behind 0), so the entry's own trigger fires. Observed on the real server: the G3 scene's filter saw five replayed lines on the v0.8.2 asset and one on v0.9.0 (the one the scene creates for itself), so the burst is gone rather than merely believed gone.
- ~~**Title-change `pane_updated` events drive a snapshot per command**~~ (observed in the Phase 1 sponsor demo, 2026-09-01): herdr emits `pane_updated` whenever a pane's terminal title changes, i.e. on every command a user runs in any pane, and the client schedules a (coalesced) `session.snapshot` for each; acceptable at the design workload (one snapshot per 500 ms at most, and the sponsor demo stayed responsive); hardening = ignore a `pane_updated` whose only delta versus the cached record is `title`/`terminal_title*`/`revision`, or resync that pane with `pane.get` instead of a full snapshot; trigger = visible lag or herdr CPU attributable to snapshot frequency, or Phase 5's UI wiring (do it then). **Closed 2026-09-04 (Phase 5, `fa07a23`):** a title-only `pane_updated` costs one cache write and emits nothing (`client_ignores_title_only_pane_updated`); ratified by merging PR #5.
- **Unanchored seen trees, not only loose blobs** (Phase 2 design review F1, extends "Alternates read-miss"): git skips the physical write for any object the alternate already has, so `HEAD^{tree}` at first sight and any fold result equal to an existing tree live only in the user's objects dir; a history rewrite + reflog expiry (30 d) + auto-gc deletes them and `read-tree <seen_tree>` fails. Acceptable because the engine checks `exists(seen_tree)` on open and before every fold and fails open (`null` seen tree, notice, refold from empty); hardening = `refs/lastcall/seen` and an overrides-tree ref in the store plus copying the tree objects in, after which store `gc` also becomes safe; trigger = any observed "seen tree missing" notice in real use.
- **Private store grows without bound and is never gc'd** (§6.1, Phase 2): one loose object per scanned version of each changed file and per accepted hunk; nothing in the store is ref-anchored, so `gc`/`prune`/`repack -d` are forbidden. Acceptable at the design workload (objects are small, alternates cover unchanged content); hardening = the ref anchors above, then `git gc --auto` on the store; trigger = a state dir above ~1 GB or a sponsor complaint.
- **Non-UTF-8 paths are pending but not acceptable** (Phase 2): paths are bytes end to end and JSON renders them lossily; a non-UTF-8 path shows as a row with a notice and every accept refuses it (over-show). Acceptable because APFS refuses to create such names and Linux repos with them are rare; hardening = base64 or `\x` escaping in the JSON and a byte-path accept API; trigger = a user report.
- **Clean filters run inside the private store** (Phase 2 design review F18): `hash-object -w` under `GIT_DIR=<store>` executes the user's clean filters (`filter=lfs` runs `git-lfs clean`, which writes under `<store>/lfs/` or fails when git-lfs is absent — the path then shows as `unreadable`). Acceptable because the result is over-show; hardening = `-c filter.<name>.required=false` plus a notice, or treating LFS pointers as collapsed; trigger = LFS-backed repos in real use.
- **Restore refuses filtered paths** (Phase 7 kickoff, review F2): a path with any `filter` attribute (LFS, user filters) cannot be restored — the store cannot promise to smudge it and writing the canonical blob would drop a pointer over the user's file; acceptable because the row still shows and accept works; hardening = run the smudge through the user's filter driver with a size cap; trigger = an LFS repo in the design workload.
- **Ledger 1.1 under a 1.0 binary keeps only the first flag** (Phase 7 kickoff, review F4): a 1.0 reader drops `flags` and re-stamps the file; the 1.1 writer's `flag` mirror preserves the first entry; acceptable because a downgrade is a deliberate act and the note survives; hardening = a minor-version check that refuses to write a newer file; trigger = a second machine sharing the state dir on an older build.
- **A restore temp file survives a crash with no later restore of that name** (Phase 7 kickoff, review F8): `.<name>.lastcall-restore-<pid>-<n>` is dropped by the scan and swept at the next restore of the same name once its pid is dead; acceptable because it is invisible in lastcall and named for what it is; hardening = a sweep at `Engine::open`; trigger = one seen in the wild.
- **Restore refuses a file whose worktree bytes are not reproducible from its blob** (Phase 7 checkpoint, verifier (a) F2; orchestrator ruling under the delegation): on a git root the restore path materialises the *current* blob through git's filters and compares it to the live bytes; under bare `* text=auto` a CRLF working file differs (git would write LF), so the restore is refused with `eol conversion is not round-trippable` and nothing is written — acceptable because the row still shows and accept works, and the alternative (normalise as `git checkout` would) silently rewrites every line ending of a file the user never touched; hardening = re-apply the working file's detected line ending to the materialised bytes when the only difference is eol; trigger = a CRLF repository in the design workload. Alt on record: normalise as `git checkout` would and document it here.
- **The standalone export file is keyed by root basename and UTC day** (Phase 7 close, verifier (b) F8): two roots with the same basename share `exports/<basename>/<date>.md` and the header line carries only the basename; the day is the engine clock's UTC date, so an evening flag in a western timezone lands in tomorrow's file; acceptable because the file is a fallback for the no-agent case and each entry names its root and timestamp; hardening = key by the root's store hash (or full path) and use the local date; trigger = a sponsor hit on a same-basename pair, or the Phase 9 design pass.
- **Keybinding config validated in the binary, not the engine** (Phase 3): `Config.keys` is an opaque name→specs map in the engine; only the TUI knows action names, so `lastcall config` and `lastcall tui` validate it. Acceptable because both surfaces fail loudly before any screen; hardening = move action names behind an engine trait if a second UI appears; trigger = a second consumer of `[keys]`.
- **Watcher event delivery is proven only where FSEvents works** (Phase 2): the polling backstops (HEAD 10 s, rescan 30 s, `--poll`) carry a host whose `fseventsd` is unhealthy; the FS-delivery integration test self-skips with a visible reason there. Acceptable because invariant 9 makes events hints; hardening = none needed; trigger = a Phase 3 UI feeling sluggish on a healthy host.
- **Atomic-rename restore orphans open file descriptors** (§6.3): an editor/agent holding the old inode open keeps writing to the orphan and its later writes vanish; acceptable because agents write-and-close and the rescan shows the restore result honestly; hardening = in-place write for files with open writers (detect via `lsof`/`fuser`); trigger = a user report of an editor buffer diverging after restore.
- **Rescan failure after a committed accept reports Err** (Phase 4, verifier (a) F4): `accept_with` runs the ledger op and the rescan in one critical section; if the rescan fails after the op committed, the caller gets `Err` although the ledger changed, so the status line says failed while the accept stood — no data loss, the next scan shows the truth. Acceptable because it needs the repo to break between two git calls. Hardening = return `Accepted` with a notice and no pile, or retry the rescan once. Trigger = any observed status/ledger disagreement. Decided in §10 "Phase 4 close-out".
- ~~**One `index.tmp` per root is shared by every lastcall process**~~ (Phase 4): `write_tree`, `tree_of_disk` and `detect_renames` build the temp index at one path under the root's store, so two instances over one root can clash, and a second instance doubles every watcher scan. Acceptable because the design workload is one TUI per machine and the ledger write itself is locked (E1). Hardening (sponsor, verbatim "we need to ensure that a deferral task is to ensure that we limit to 1 instance or otherwise handle this gracefully with N-instances somehow") = either a per-state-dir instance lock with a clear message on the second launch, or graceful N-instance operation (per-process temp-index suffix, cooperative store locking, one watcher per root). Trigger = the Phase 5 kickoff (which also evaluates one pane per herdr workspace) must rule which. Decided in §10 "Phase 4 close-out". **Closed 2026-09-04 (Phase 5 kickoff ruling 2, worker 5a):** graceful N instances — per-process `index.<pid>.tmp` swept on open (own pid unconditional, others after 1 h), shared persistent index, `LOCK_RETRIES` 40 × 50 ms with `ledger busy in <root> — try again` on exhaustion (`engine_two_engines_over_one_root_keep_both_accepts`, `engine_two_engines_scan_one_root_concurrently_and_agree`, `engine_accept_under_a_held_ledger_lock_is_lock_busy_not_a_refusal`, `store_open_sweeps_a_stale_temp_index_left_by_a_dead_process`); ratified by merging PR #5.
- ~~**Engine open and scan are serial and git-spawn bound**~~ (Phase 4 bench S1): 29 + 19 spawns per root at ≈13 ms, roots opened and scanned one after another; acceptable only until the Phase 5 entry obligation lands (first deliverable of Phase 5, §8); baseline `docs/dev/bench.md` S1; trigger = Phase 5 kickoff. Decided in §10 "Phase 4 close-out". **Closed 2026-09-04 (Phase 5 entry obligation, worker 5a):** bounded pool (`min(available_parallelism, 8)`), one `config --list -z` and one batched `rev-parse` per root — open 29 → 11 spawns per root, scan 19 → 17 (edit+add+delete shape); S1 first frame 56,568 → 8,762 ms, S1h (50 × 80) 4,782 ms (`docs/dev/bench.md`); ratified by merging PR #5.
- **Review-ready ack is per lastcall process** (Phase 5 kickoff, 2026-09-04): several instances over one session each derive the flag from the same herdr events and each keeps its own ack, so acking in one leaves the flag lit in the others until `g` (which clears herdr's own seen flag) or their own ack; hardening = a shared ack file per root store read on every re-derive; trigger = the sponsor finds a stale flag in a second instance annoying.
- ~~**Compat workflow unexecuted until its first green run**~~ (Phase 5 close-out, 2026-09-04): `herdr-compat.yml`, the consumed-surface fixture and the dedupe rule are committed and the schema test is green locally, but no GitHub run exists before the push; acceptable because the `pull_request` trigger runs it when PR #5 opens and the kickoff planned for one red run; hardening = none — the first green run link in the PR merge note closes this entry and the Gate 5 row; trigger = that run (or a red one, which is triaged as drift or a workflow defect before merge). **Closed 2026-09-04:** the PR #5 `pull_request` run was green on the first attempt (https://github.com/aarontimko/lastcall/actions/runs/33896045805; latest v0.8.2, four tests, no skip).
- **A never-quiet file inside a root costs a scan every 3 s under the debounce cap** (Phase 6 kickoff, 2026-09-05): an app logging into the repo keeps that root's burst alive, so the cap scans it about every 3.2 s (~17 git processes) for as long as the writer runs, and the changed line count redraws each time; acceptable because today the same file starves the root's *review* entirely until the 30 s backstop, the cost is a fraction of a core per such root, and `ignore_globs` scopes the watcher away from it; hardening = a per-file event-rate detector that auto-suggests an ignore glob in a notice, or excluding a row whose only change is appended lines from the redraw; trigger = a sponsor-observed CPU cost or a redraw-every-3-s complaint in real use. Decided at the Phase 6 kickoff (§10). **Built in Phase 6** (`watcher::schedule`/`scanned`, B12, Amendment v1.6).
- **Scans started outside the watcher loop do not clear the cap window** (Phase 6 close-out, verifier (a) F7): a TUI `r` refresh or a post-accept scan runs through `watcher::blocking`, so a burst window opened before it can still fire its capped scan once — one redundant scan, then the window resets; acceptable because it is bounded and the scan is cheap; hardening = route those scans through the loop's `scanned` hook; trigger = a redundant-scan report in real use. Same class as the `HeadChange` scan leaving `due` (one extra scan at most).
- **An external editor's session blesses whatever is on disk when the user confirms** (Phase 8 kickoff, ruling P1 + confirm, 2026-09-06): lastcall cannot tell who wrote the bytes between `shift-i` and the editor's exit, so an agent's write during the session is set as the override if the user answers the return confirm with Enter without noticing; acceptable because the pre-launch CAS refuses a file that moved before the editor opened, the editor's own changed-on-disk warning fires during the session, and the confirm names the path on return; hardening = compare the return content against the editor's own save (mtime/inode heuristics) or watch the file during the suspend and word the confirm differently after more than one write burst; trigger = an agent and the human editing one file concurrently becoming a workflow; decided in §10 2026-09-06.
- **An external `SIGQUIT` is swallowed for the process lifetime** (Phase 8 close-out, verifier (b) F5, 2026-09-06): registering tokio's `quit()` stream so a `^\` typed at `$EDITOR` cannot core-dump lastcall installs a handler that also absorbs a `kill -QUIT` sent from outside; acceptable because the alternative is `SIG_IGN`/`pre_exec`, both unsafe under the workspace's `unsafe_code = "forbid"`, `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` still stop the process, and a core dump of a TUI is not a workflow anyone has asked for; hardening = read the stream and exit on a quit that arrives while no editor is running; trigger = a sponsor need for `kill -QUIT` semantics.
- ~~**The sponsor's ledger and the orchestrator's disagree about this repo**~~ **CLOSED 2026-09-07 (Phase 9a freeze; §10):** the sponsor's runs set `LASTCALL_STATE_DIR=$(mktemp -d)`, so they wrote to throwaway stores under `$TMPDIR`, never to `~/.local/state/lastcall`; no bug; the hardening (`status --json` `state_dir`/`store`/`ledger_written_at`, `lastcall status` printing `state dir:` first) ships in 9a. Original entry: (Gate 8 sponsor run, 2026-09-06): with the same `HOME` and state dir (`~/.local/state/lastcall`, no config file) the sponsor's launch listed this repo as clean while the orchestrator's `lastcall watch --json` over the same state dir showed it with pending rows against a 2026-09-04 ledger; the sponsor had accepted everything in it earlier in the run and saw it clean, and the orchestrator's scans came *after* that — as if the two runs wrote to different stores, which the printed `state_dir` says they did not; unexplained; acceptable because every scan reads the ledger it is given and nothing here touches review correctness; hardening = a `lastcall status --json` line naming the ledger's last-write time and the store hash so two runs can be compared; trigger = the Phase 9 kickoff, before any scoping or listing change is built on top.
- **`ExportContext.attribution` is always `None`** (Phase 8 close-out, worker #2 needs-a-ruling 2, 2026-09-06): Phase 7's engine docs said Phase 8 would provide the herdr attribution on a flag export; no phase's deliverable list contains it, so the field is carried empty; acceptable because the export names the path, hunk and note and the receiving agent is the one that wrote the change; hardening = fill it from the herdr pane/agent that last wrote the file (the §6.8 attribution layer's first consumer); trigger = the Phase 9 kickoff ruling on whether it ships in v0.1.0.
- **A held mouse press defers the signal arm by one pass** (Phase 6 close-out, verifier (b) F6): when the drain hands a press back to the next iteration, that iteration folds it without entering `select!`, so a pending SIGTERM is seen one pass later; acceptable because a pass is one frame; hardening = poll the signal in the held branch; trigger = none expected.
- **Undo stack and snooze are dropped by a `v0.1.0` binary's first write** (Phase 10 kickoff, 2026-09-13): the ledger stays schema 1.1 and `deny_unknown_fields` is off, so an older binary over the same state directory loads and then rewrites the file without `undo` and `snoozed_until`; acceptable because two versions over one state directory is a transient (the `update` path replaces the binary in place) and the loss is a convenience, never review state; hardening = a schema bump with a reader for both; trigger = a user report of a lost undo after downgrading.
- **The first-launch tour may write the user's config file** (Phase 10 kickoff, 2026-09-13): the only writer of `config.toml` in the tree, limited to one key per choice card, format-preserving through `toml_edit`, tmp + rename, refusing (and showing the line to paste) when the file does not parse; acceptable because the write is opt-in per card and covered by a byte-diff test; hardening = a `lastcall config set` command if a second writer is ever wanted; trigger = a second key that wants writing.

## Amendments

(log entries take the form: version, date, phase N, change, reason it was unknowable at design time)

- **v1.1 — 2026-09-01 — Phase 1 — §5 herdr surface — PROPOSED; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 1 PR.** Changes: (1) §5.2: the released v0.8.2 answers protocol **20**, not 21; the client accepts `[20, 21]`; §4.6 range updated. (2) §5.3: on v0.8.2, lifecycle subscriptions replay the whole event ring on connect (sequence 0 start); per-pane subscriptions do not; the client treats the replay burst as hints only and never derives the review-ready indicator from a replayed lifecycle event. (3) §5.4/§6.6, additive: a per-pane status connection is also closed at resync when its pane is no longer agent-bearing (agent released), not only on `pane_closed`/`pane_exited` — keeps the "one connection per agent-bearing pane" sizing honest. Why unknowable at design time: §5 was verified against master `5158ada`, believed to equal the v0.8.2 release because `Cargo.toml` still said 0.8.2; the tag was 55 commits behind master and the difference only surfaced when the worker's integration test ran the published binary. Standing rule from this: **every §5 claim is verified against the release tag we pin, never master**; the Phase 5 circle-back re-verifies against the then-pinned tag.
- **v1.2 — 2026-09-02 — Phase 2 — §6.1/§6.4/§6.5 editorial and additive — RATIFIED by the sponsor merging PR #2 (`ba62907`, 2026-09-02).** Changes: (1) §6.5: pending, conflict and in-progress state are computed from the private index, `ls-files -u`, and the git-dir files; `git status` (and `diff`, `add`, `checkout`, `stash`, anything that can take the user's `index.lock`) is **never run** on the user's repository — porcelain v2 remains the reference *semantics* only. (2) §6.1: the private store is never `gc`'d, pruned or repacked (nothing in it is ref-anchored); an orphan object from a crash is simply left in place; an unresolvable seen tree opens as `null` (fail open). (3) §6.1, additive files under `repos/<repo-hash>/`: `index.tree` (the tree the private index was seeded from), `index.tmp` (the fold's temp index), `lock` (advisory `flock`; every ledger read-modify-write holds it, re-reads, then writes tmp + fsync + rename). (4) §6.1 config keys copied into the store at every open now include `info/attributes`, and the store forces `core.fsmonitor=false`, `core.untrackedCache=false`, `core.splitIndex=false` (a user's global `core.fsmonitor=true` otherwise hangs the private-index refresh). (5) §6.4: remote-tracking refs are a classification input — a fetch or push may change `upstream`/`mixed` labels without HEAD moving. (6) §6.5, draft roots: relative `draft_dirs` globs match under parent dirs and under discovered git roots (§10 ruling). (7) New surfaces: `lastcall status [--json] [--root <path>…]` emits `status_version: 1` (schema in `docs/dev/engine.md`; an unresolvable `--root` exits 1); `lastcall watch [--json] [--exit-after <secs>] [--poll <secs>]` streams engine events. (8) §6.4 D6: a skip-worktree path that is absent from disk is never a deletion; one that is present is a normal candidate. Why unknowable at design time: §6.1/§6.5 were drafted before the harness and the build showed that git skips writes for alternate-resident objects, that the store inherits the user's global config (fsmonitor), and that `git status` on the user's repo is both unnecessary and a lock hazard.
- **v1.3 — 2026-09-02 — Phase 3 — §6.1 additive config key and `status --json` additive field — RATIFIED by the sponsor merging PR #3 (`1d8f998`, 2026-09-02).** Changes: (1) §6.1 `config.toml` gains a `[keys]` table: `<action> = "<key>"` or `["<key>", …]`, replacing that action's default bindings; key specs are modifier prefixes `ctrl-`/`alt-`/`shift-` plus a character or a named key; unknown action names, unparsable specs and duplicate bindings are load errors reported by `lastcall config` and by `lastcall tui` before it takes the terminal (sponsor ruling, §10 2026-09-02; §6.7's "config-stubbed" keybindings become real). (2) `status_version: 1` gains a per-root `remote` field: the `org/repo` slug of `remote.origin.url` (read with `git config --get`, not `git remote get-url`, which the read-only allowlist lacks — equivalent except under `insteadOf`), `null` for no remote or a local-path origin. Why unknowable at design time: §6.1 froze the v1 key list before any UI existed, and the review found that a stable golden needs local origins to render as no remote.
- **v1.4 — 2026-09-02 — Phase 4 — `status --json` additive field `omitted` (unscanned changed paths above the row cap; `status_version` stays 1); §11 non-UTF-8 entry reworded (the accept-all fold has no UTF-8 check: a non-UTF-8 path is refused by every accept scope, over-showing); §6.4 `remote` documented as read via `config --get remote.origin.url` — PROPOSED; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 4 PR.** Unknowable at design time: the row cap and its accounting were ruled by the performance amendment, and the rename/remote details surfaced in the Phase 3–4 builds.
- **v1.5 — 2026-09-03 — Phase 5 — §6.3 "accept hunk" row: the CAS clause becomes "CAS: refused if the path's baseline moved since the render (the live hash is not checked: the override is baseline + the accepted hunk, so an edit elsewhere in the file stays pending and a rewrite of the accepted lines shows as a new hunk)"; §6.1 additive config keys `herdr.toast` and `herdr.scope` — RATIFIED by the sponsor merging PR #5 (`839da62`, 2026-09-04).** Unknowable at design time: the live-hash check read as a safety property until the Phase 4 sponsor run showed it refusing hunk accepts every time the agent kept typing elsewhere in the file (§10, 2026-09-03 ruling 1).
- **v1.6 — 2026-09-05 — Phase 6 — §6.5 debounce sentence becomes "debounced per root on the trailing edge (750 ms of quiet) with a 3 s cap from the first event of a burst, both hardcoded (Q6)"; §6.1's Q6 note gains the cap; scenario B12 added to `01-scenarios.md` — PROPOSED; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 6 PR.** Unknowable at design time: trailing-edge-only read as the reviewer-friendly rule (one coalesced pile per burst) until the Phase 5 sponsor run showed a never-quiet writer starving every other file in its root, and the kickoff walk-through named the realistic case — an app at debug logging appending inside the repo (§10 2026-09-05 ruling 2).
- **v1.7 — 2026-09-05 — Phase 7 — §6.2 ledger schema 1.1: `overrides[p].flags` (a queue of flags, each optionally carrying the rendered `hunk {index, header, text}`), written beside a `flag` mirror of the first entry for 1.0 readers; `flag` is `null` never omitted; every write stamps `1.1`; `status --json` gains an additive per-row `flags` list (`status_version` stays 1) — PROPOSED by the worker's checkpoint report; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 7 PR.** Unknowable at design time: v1.0 froze one flag per path before the sponsor's sketch of the feature (§10 2026-09-05 pre-kickoff) asked for queued, per-hunk flags that stay readable after the file moves on (design review F14) and for a 1.0 binary to keep the note on a downgrade (F4).

- **v1.8 — 2026-09-06 — Phase 8 — §6.3 "editor save (phase 8)" row gains the external-editor sentence (on `$EDITOR` exit the file is rehashed; a changed regular file is blessed only after the user confirms; absent/symlink/declined → pending — a confirmed exception to invariant 3); §6.2 `overrides[p].flags[*]` gains the optional `summary {hunks, added, deleted}` written for whole-file flags (additive; `SCHEMA_VERSION` unchanged; a 1.1 reader without the field prints no summary line) — PROPOSED at the Phase 8 kickoff; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 8 PR.** Unknowable at design time: the frozen row assumed the tool sees every save, which is true only of its own editor; an external editor's save is invisible, so the session, confirmed by the user, has to stand in for the CAS. The summary field came from the Gate 7 sponsor run (a whole-file flag arrived with no measure of the file's delta).
- **v1.9 — 2026-09-07 — Phase 9a — §6.7 left nav: repos are **always listed** (every root under the parent, with or without pending files; an empty repo is a name-and-branch row) and a toggle (`t`, `hide_empty` action) hides the ones with nothing pending and no attention flag; §6.7 interaction: "accepting a repo's last file selects the repo row (with empty repos hidden, the repo collapses out of the nav and the entry that takes its place is selected)"; §6.1 additive key `hide_empty_repos` (bool, default `false`); `status --json` additive top-level `state_dir` and per-root `store`, `ledger_written_at` (`status_version` stays 1) — PROPOSED at the Phase 9a kickoff; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 9a PR (PR #9, 2026-09-10, `ba7c60b`).** Unknowable at design time: §6.7's "appearing only when they have pending files" was written before the sponsor used the tool over twenty clones from a parent directory and found that a repo vanishing on its last accept reads as loss of place, not as progress (Gate 8 run (ii), then the 2026-09-07 ruling); the store fields come from the Gate 8 ledger discrepancy, which was only diagnosable by listing temp directories by hand.
- **v1.10 — 2026-09-11 — Phase 9b — item 2 §6.1: the `[update]` table (`check = true|false`, default `true`; unknown keys a load error like every table) and the state-dir file `update-check.json` (`{checked_at, latest, seen_version}`, the once-a-day check's stamp); item 4 §8 Gate 9 wording: the docs item drops the sidebar-token snippet (P11, the token is post-v1), the `update` evidence reads "install of the rc, then `update` to the final tag; the container smoke against a local server is the automated proxy", the perf clauses read as the 2026-09-11 §10 entry delivers them (a target per scenario from runs D–F recorded with its Phase 4 baseline, the row cap pinned by unit tests plus a snapshot, run G on one quiet machine); item 5 §5.2 / §4.6: protocol 22 accepted from the v0.9.0 pin (`SUPPORTED_PROTOCOLS = [20, 21, 22]`; the consumed surface unchanged; the schema fixture's diff additive, four entries) — PROPOSED at the Phase 9b build; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 9b PR.** Item 3 (§6.6, P6) stays parked with the §3.5 circle-back. `status_version` stays 1. §5's field contract is untouched: item 5 widens the accepted range, it does not change what is read.
- **v1.11 — 2026-09-13 — Phase 10 — item 1 §6.2: two additive ledger fields under schema 1.1, `undo` (a stack of at most 20 entries `{op, at, paths: {<path>: {baseline: <oid>|null, mode}}}`, each path's baseline as §6.2's resolution answered it immediately before the accept, pushed in the same write as the accept; `compact`, restore and flag push nothing) and `snoozed_until` (an ISO-8601 instant or `null`; an expired value reads as `null` and is cleared on the next write). Item 2 §6.1: a top-level state-dir file `first-launch.json` (`{shown_at, version}`, written when the tour is dismissed; absent means "show"), and the one sanctioned config writer: the tour may set exactly `hide_empty_repos = true`, `[herdr] scope = "all"` or (item 6, 2026-09-14) `search_depth = 2`, format-preserving, tmp + rename, creating the file when absent. Item 3 §6.3: three rows, "undo" (pop the top entry; per path restore the recorded baseline through the same override rule every accept uses, so a baseline equal to the seen tree drops the override and a null on a tree-less path resolves to Empty again, never Absent; flags preserved; never touches the working tree; an empty stack is a refusal), "snooze" (`snoozed_until = now + N days`, 1 ≤ N ≤ 365) and "unsnooze" (`null`). Item 4 §6.7: the first-launch overlay (three cards, four from 2026-09-14 with the `Where lastcall looks` card second, fixed keys, shown once the launch hold and the scope verdict are past, not under 60×14); `z` undo; `s` on a repository row opens the day-count modal, `s` on a shown snoozed repository wakes it, `shift-s` shows snoozed; a snoozed repository is not listed unless its herdr flag has attention (the `t` exception) or the session shows snoozed; the bottom line adds `· N snoozed (S shows)`. Item 4, extended 2026-09-14 (§10 that day): four rebindable list actions `nav_top`, `nav_bottom`, `nav_prev_root`, `nav_next_root` (`Home`, `End`, `Alt-Up` or `{`, `Alt-Down` or `}`), and `accept_file` (`A`) folds a repository from its row or a group entry while `accept` (`a`) there refuses with a status naming the `accept_file` key (supersedes the Phase 4 per-root fold on `a`). Item 5 `status --json`: additive per-root `undo` (stack depth) and `snoozed_until`; `status_version` stays 1. Item 6 (added 2026-09-14, deliverable 8, sponsor ruling "1 alt") §6.1: `search_depth` (1 to 4, default 1); at 2 and above discovery walks the plain folders below each parent, never entering a repository or a dependency folder (`.git`, `node_modules`, `target`, `.venv`, `vendor`), and lists the linked worktrees kept inside each listed repository through one `git worktree list --porcelain` per root, filed where that repository is filed and badged `WorktreeOf`; depth 1 is the old rule unchanged; scenarios D12 and D13 join `01-scenarios.md` under this amendment. — RATIFIED by the sponsor merging PR #25 (`341dde4`, 2026-09-14).** Unknowable at design time: the three items are the sponsor's own findings from using the released product; undo in particular depends on the compaction and baseline-resolution behaviour settled in Phases 4 and 7.
- **v1.12 — 2026-09-15 — Phase 11 — §6.2: schema 1.2, additive under major 1: top-level `seen_branch` (the branch the record in force belongs to; tri-state on the wire: absent means written by a 1.1 binary, `null` means detached or draft) and `branches` (branch name → parked record `{seen_tree, seen_at, overrides, undo, parked_at}`, omitted when empty); one record per branch the repository has been checked out on while lastcall watched, the record in force selected by the branch name in `<git_dir>/HEAD` at every scan, head inspection and open; first sight of a branch is a copy of the record just left, then, when the two tips share history, a fold to their merge-base (the arrived-on tip when it is behind, the left tip when it is ahead, the common ancestor when they have diverged) taking the merge-base's entry for a path differing between the left tip and the merge-base only when the record's baseline for it equals the left branch's tip entry and the merge-base's entry is already seen state, meaning the merge-base is reachable from the commit the root was first sighted at (`first_sight_head`, a new top-level field set once at first sight, omitted when unknown) or some other record already parked composes that entry as its baseline (a record with no seen tree and no override for the path composes nothing) (flag-only overrides kept, gitlinks skipped; every other differing path keeps the copied baseline and over-shows; the arrived-on branch's own commits since the merge-base always show); parked records pruned when their ref is gone (prune skipped on error); the current branch's rename re-labels the record in force; detached, unborn and in-progress states never switch; an accept staged under another branch is refused with a notice and its staged work dropped; compaction, undo and flags scoped to the record in force. §6.4 "Branch switch" and "HEAD is never consulted": the branch name selects the record and ancestry is a one-time first-sight input, like `HEAD^{tree}` at a root's first sight; the first-sight notice `switched A → B: first time here, seen state carried from A; N files pending` when the head moved, `(same commit)` otherwise. `status --json`: additive `seen_branch` and `parked_branches`, `status_version` 1. Scenarios D14 to D26 and the seen-oracle property test — RATIFIED by the sponsor merging PR #27 (`d668984`, 2026-09-17).** Unknowable at design time: the deferral entry assumed in-place hops would be brief; the sponsor's daily flow (an unattended run on a generated branch, then the return) made the switch the common case, and the fold is what the reported case needed beyond the ruled copy; the sponsor's own gate run then showed the ancestor form of the fold failing on a branch cut from `main` inside one debounce window, and the merge-base form replaced it (§10 2026-09-15); a third verifier then showed the merge-base form baselining versions that had never been on a screen, and the seen-state target replaced its second guard (§10 2026-09-16).
- **v1.13 — 2026-09-17 — Phase 12 — §6.1: a plain `draft_dirs` entry reviews the files directly inside the matched folder and nothing below; the `/**` suffix reviews the tree; a draft root never reads a regular file at or above `collapse_size_bytes`, counting the unrecorded ones in one notice and showing a recorded one that changed as an unread row; an enclosing root excludes what a draft root inside it reads; the glob walk under each base goes as deep as the entry has path components and `*` never crosses `/`; new key `draft_dir_parents` (0 to 4, default 1) prefixing a draft root's name. §6.2: a draft root's first sight covers its scope; a record holding paths outside the scope by shape is trimmed at the next scan, with a notice and no undo entry. `01-scenarios.md`: F4 to F9 added. **PROPOSED**; ratified by the sponsor merging the Phase 12 PR.**
